# AHTW — Complete Combat, Skills & Encounter-Resolution Dossier

**Audience:** combat designer, skill designer, enemy/encounter designer, balance reviewer, gameplay programmer, QA.
**Purpose:** one self-contained review and implementation dossier containing the approved combat model, deterministic resolution contract, effect lifecycle, full 67-record skill catalog, scaling definitions, semantic dispositions, reconciliation decisions, and open validation work.
**Status:** semantic skill design/reconciliation is closed at the documented baseline; the normalized catalog is a candidate requiring runtime integration, data validation, encounter balance, and QA. Numbers remain provisional where the source says they are provisional. This document does not claim runtime systems or tests already exist.

## Authority and safe implementation rules
- This dossier contains the current source documents and structured skill data verbatim for offline review.
- Preserve stable IDs, explicit target/outcome contracts, resource costs, scaling-profile references, status effect identifiers, and encounter permissions.
- If a field is missing, ambiguous, contradictory, or fails validation, flag it for resolution; do not silently invent a value.
- Semantic identity matters: implementation templates may be shared, but skills should not be collapsed merely because their effects appear similar.
- The game is deterministic by design. The same state and inputs should resolve through the same phase order and application order.
- All catalog numeric values described as provisional must be balanced and validated in runtime context before being treated as final.

## Locked combat model
- **Vitality** is the primary defeat meter.
- **Strain** is a secondary pressure meter supporting thresholds, alternate outcomes, reactive mechanics, and Overflow.
- **Command** is generated by directive/manipulation actions and spent to alter behavior, reduce resistance, or prepare Break.
- **Resolve** is generated through deliberate exposure/risk and enables responses and alternate finish paths.
- **Pressure** is generated by exploiting weaknesses, vulnerable states, or unstable targets; it improves damage/debuffs.
- **Fortitude** is generated by receiving damage, resisting pressure, or surviving critical situations; it is spent for defense, retaliation, and survival.
- Keep damage channels distinct. A skill may affect Vitality, Strain, both, or convert one result into another only when explicitly defined.
- Core keywords include **Exploit, Consume, Chain, Threshold, Overflow**.
- Skill targeting is skill-defined; combat is not based on a grid or independent range system.
- Builds emerge from skills, stats, equipment, passives, modifications, and choices rather than rigid classes.
- Readable enemy intent and defensive/reactive responses should be used where designed.
- Encounters can resolve through defeat, disable, capture, yield, retreat, or objective completion. A finisher must only produce outcomes permitted by that encounter; it must not imply universal Defeat or global victory.
- One Active skill consumes one action point. Skills do not grant extra actions unless an explicit, validated rule supersedes this baseline.
- Alya-only `CAPABILITY_P_GROWTH` must be validated before costs are committed.
- Equivalent effects follow the locked strongest-effect policy unless an explicit exception is documented.

## Deterministic turn and effect rules
- Use one canonical source of truth for stats and effects; do not duplicate modifiers across equipment, statuses, and cached state.
- Effects track source, target, start rule, duration, stacks, strength, tags, and removal conditions.
- At the affected actor's turn start, resolve start-of-turn effects in deterministic application order; tick and decrement duration according to the canonical lifecycle, then expire effects when due.
- Effects are applied in a stable order. Same/equivalent effects follow the strongest-equivalent rule; different stacks are computed in the order inflicted only where the contract defines distinct stacks.
- Removing equipment immediately removes effects granted solely by that equipment.
- Overflow triggers at most once per affected actor per resolved action on threshold crossing, has a recursion guard, and threshold lock removal re-evaluates the threshold.
- Validate all event ordering and edge cases with automated tests before asserting the implementation matches the contract.

## Catalog scope
- 67 canonical skill records.
- Canonical CSV plus normalized JSON and referenced scaling profiles are included in full below.
- Progression skill-point costs: Tier 1 = 1, Tier 2 = 2, Tier 3 = 3.
- Tier gates: Tier 1 has no act gate; Tier 2 unlocks after Act I; Tier 3 unlocks after Act II.
- Catalog status: normalized candidate; runtime integration and encounter balance QA remain pending.

## Combat review pack

# AHTW — Combat & Skills Review Pack

**Audience:** combat designer, skill designer, enemy/encounter designer, balance reviewer.  
**Purpose:** summarize the approved combat model and skill-catalog decisions.

## 1. Combat identity

Combat is turn-based, deterministic in its resolution order, and driven by skill-defined targeting rather than grid position or independent range. Builds emerge from skills, stats, equipment, passives, modifications and choices; the game does not require rigid classes.

The player should be able to read enemy intent where designed and use defensive/reactive actions to respond. Encounters can require more than reducing enemy health: outcomes may include defeat, disable, capture, yield, retreat, or objective completion.

## 2. Core meters and tactical resources

- **Vitality:** primary defeat meter.
- **Strain:** secondary pressure meter; supports thresholds, alternate outcomes, reactive mechanics and Overflow.
- **Command:** generated by directive/manipulation actions; spent to alter behavior, reduce resistance or prepare Break.
- **Resolve:** generated through deliberate exposure/risk; enables responses and alternate finish paths.
- **Pressure:** generated by exploiting weaknesses, vulnerable states or unstable targets; improves damage/debuffs.
- **Fortitude:** generated by receiving damage, resisting pressure or surviving critical situations; spent for defense, retaliation and survival.

Canonical vocabulary is neutral and mechanical. Thematic labels are presentation/localization and must not redefine mechanics.

## 3. Keywords and action model

Core keywords include **Exploit, Consume, Chain, Threshold, Overflow**. Skills may share implementation templates without losing distinct identities when their scaling, requirements, costs, synergies or tactical hooks differ.

- Validate requirements and resource costs before committing an action.
- Insufficient resources make an action unavailable.
- An atomic action completes its declared effect/reaction pipeline before terminal state is committed.
- Reactions/chains/threshold checks follow deterministic order.
- Initiative is randomized once at encounter start, with Agility modifying the initial result; turn order then remains fixed.
- Surrendered and Incapacitated are distinct states, but both prevent acting and count as out of active combat.
- Combat ends when every member of one side is Surrendered and/or Incapacitated.
- AI preserves intent/action if a target becomes invalid, retargeting where possible and otherwise using an explicit fallback.

## 4. Effects and passives

Effects have definitions and runtime instances, with source, target, start/duration, stacks, strength, tags and removal rules. Strongest equivalent effect wins by default unless explicit data overrides stacking. Equipment-derived effects disappear immediately when their granting equipment is removed; cursed equipment follows its explicit curse contract.

Passives use declared categories such as CorePassive, BuildPassive and ConditionalPassive. Derived stats recalculate after relevant mutations. State mutations commit before immutable domain events publish.

## 5. Skill catalog and approved migration

- Current canonical skill catalog: **67 records**.
- Primary catalog: `data/skills/skills.csv`; complex definitions: `data/skills/skill_definitions.json`.
- Stable skill IDs must be preserved unless a reviewed migration plan explicitly changes them.
- H-gauge semantics migrate to Strain where applicable; Health Damage maps to damage to Vitality, but each effect must be checked against its intended meaning.
- P-growth is a Capability/Transformation that enables dependent skills.
- Tiers represent both unlock/progression and expected power.
- Unlocks combine Skill Points, prerequisites and progression gates.
- Preserve the legacy fantasy/intention, but merge or remove only genuine mechanical redundancy. Enslave was consolidated as an alias of Absolute Control in the normalized catalog.

Do not invent new skills to meet a quota or copy complex mechanics into vertical-slice data when a canonical skill definition already exists.

## 6. Encounter design baseline

Full-game content lock: 35 enemy archetypes, 9 major bosses, 8 special encounters. This is a content inventory, not a claim that every encounter is tuned or runtime-tested.

Act I introduces:
- **Frayed Husk:** slow direct pressure; teaches target selection.
- **Threadling Swarm:** faster Strain/pressure application.
- **Hollow Warden:** defensive elite with visible intent; rewards control/preparation.
- **Root-Knot Anchor:** objective entity; pressure continues until the sluice objective is completed.

The climax is objective-based: stabilize the diversion, sever the active filament, and secure the gate. Simply eliminating enemies is insufficient.

## 7. Balance and QA status

Catalog normalization and structural validation are documented. Do not claim actual runtime balance or Unity playtest completion from a static catalog check. Numerical values, encounter difficulty, skill synergies, AI behavior and edge-case interactions require implementation-level tests and playtesting.

Balance review should include:
- every skill's action economy, cost, targeting, requirements and effect semantics;
- Strain thresholds and alternate outcomes;
- resource generation/spending loops;
- control and defensive response counterplay;
- multi-target and objective encounter behavior;
- progression/unlock feasibility;
- AI fallback and terminal-state ordering;
- interactions with equipment, passives and temporary effects.

## 8. Review checklist

- Does each skill have a distinct tactical reason to exist?
- Are costs, targeting, requirements, effects and scaling explicit?
- Does a skill's thematic label match its actual effect without redefining it?
- Are alternate combat outcomes handled intentionally?
- Can objective encounters be completed through the intended actions?
- Are resource loops exploitable or degenerate?
- Are effects and reactions ordered deterministically?
- Are numeric claims marked provisional until runtime testing?

## Combat implementation contract

# Combat

## Meters
Vitality: primary defeat meter.
Strain: secondary pressure meter. It supports thresholds, alternate outcomes, reactive mechanics, and Overflow.

Keep damage channels distinct. A skill may affect Vitality, Strain, both, or convert one result into another when explicitly defined.

## Shared resources
Command: target-control resource generated by directive/manipulation actions; spent to alter behavior, reduce resistance, or prepare a Break.
Resolve: reactive resource generated through deliberate exposure or risk; enables responses and alternate finish paths.
Pressure: generated by exploiting weaknesses, vulnerable states, or unstable targets; improves damage and debuffs.
Fortitude: generated by receiving damage, resisting pressure, or surviving critical situations; spent for defense, retaliation, or survival.

## Skill keywords
Exploit: gains conditional bonuses against matching tags/states.
Consume: spends stacks/resources for a stronger effect.
Chain: changes when repeated or sequenced.
Threshold: changes based on a meter boundary.
Overflow: interacts with Strain above its normal threshold.

## Build identity
Do not use rigid classes. Build identity emerges from skill tags, stats, equipment, passives, modifications, and choices.

## Enemy difficulty
Prefer authored power bands plus behavioral variation, encounter composition, modifiers, and objectives. Do not universally scale all enemies to player level.

## Enemy intent
Enemies may expose intended actions. Defensive and reactive skills can inspect or respond to intent.

## Outcome resolution
Combat must support Defeat, Disable, Capture, Yield, Retreat, and ObjectiveComplete. Outcome rules are encounter-defined rather than globally forcing elimination.

## Status-effect lifecycle contract

# Status Effects and Lifecycle

## Source of truth
Stats and effects must have one canonical source. Do not duplicate the same modifier in equipment, status, and cached state.

## Effect lifecycle
An effect has source, target, start rule, duration, stacks, strength, tags, and removal conditions.

At the beginning of the affected character's turn:
1. evaluate start-of-turn behavior;
2. decrement duration when the effect uses turn duration;
3. expire the effect immediately when its duration reaches its expiration condition;
4. recompute derived state if required.

## Stack policy
Different effects are processed independently in application order. Equivalent effects keep only the strongest active instance unless the effect definition explicitly allows stacking.

## Equipment rule
Removing equipment immediately removes every effect granted solely by that equipment. Recalculate derived stats immediately.

## Passive categories
CorePassive: persistent identity behavior.
BuildPassive: learned or selected build behavior.
ConditionalPassive: equipment, state, encounter, or context dependent.

## Implementation events
Effect application and removal should emit deterministic events so UI, logs, triggers, and reactions can subscribe without duplicating logic.

## Deterministic turn-resolution contract

# Turn Resolution Contract

This document defines the canonical order for turn-based encounter resolution.

## Turn phases
1. Select next active actor according to initiative/order rules.
2. Emit TurnStarted(actorId).
3. Resolve start-of-turn effects in deterministic application order.
4. For each affected effect using turn duration, decrement duration after its start behavior resolves.
5. Remove effects whose expiration condition is now satisfied; emit EffectRemoved.
6. Recalculate affected derived state.
7. If the actor is unable to act, resolve the defined skip/forced-action behavior and continue to phase 12.
8. Refresh or expose actor intent when the actor is AI-controlled.
9. Select an action; validate targeting, conditions, cooldown, costs, and gate requirements.
10. Commit costs, then resolve the action through its declared effect sequence.
11. Resolve triggered reactions, chains, thresholds, resource generation, and immediate outcome checks in deterministic order.
12. Resolve end-of-turn effects and emit TurnEnded(actorId).
13. Evaluate encounter terminal conditions. If none apply, advance to the next actor.

## Action resolution
An action is atomic from the perspective of validation: invalid actions must not partially spend costs or mutate state.

Recommended sequence:
Validate -> CommitCosts -> ApplyPrimaryEffects -> ApplySecondaryEffects -> EvaluateTriggers -> RecalculateDerivedState -> EmitEvents -> EvaluateOutcome.

## Ordering rules
- Application order is the default tie-breaker for equivalent priorities.
- Explicit priority overrides application order only when declared by data.
- Reactions created by an event resolve after the event's originating mutation unless explicitly marked interrupting.
- Terminal encounter outcomes stop further ordinary action resolution after the current atomic action completes.

## Required event payload minimum
Every turn/action event should include encounterId, actorId, sequenceNumber, eventType, and enough references to reproduce the state transition in logs.

## Determinism
Given identical canonical state, action selection, random seed/state, content version, and event ordering rules, resolution must produce identical canonical results.

## Implementation-spec orientation

# Implementation Specification Layer

This directory is a self-contained, neutral implementation specification. A consumer must be able to design and implement the planned systems using this directory alone, without consulting repository history, narrative documents, thematic source material, or external project context.

It describes systems through abstract gameplay concepts, data contracts, state machines, resources, progression, events, deterministic ordering, and ownership boundaries.

## Reading order
1. CORE_MODEL.md
2. DATA_MODEL.md
3. CONTENT_CONTRACTS.md
4. STATE_MACHINES.md
5. COMBAT.md, TURN_RESOLUTION.md, STATUS_EFFECTS.md, SKILL_CATALOG.md
6. ECONOMY.md, ITEMS_AND_EQUIPMENT.md, BUILDINGS.md, COMMUNITY.md
7. EXPLORATION.md, PROCEDURAL_GENERATION.md
8. PROGRESSION_AND_GATING.md, REWARDS.md, DIRECTION_FEEDBACK.md
9. INTEGRATION_CONTRACTS.md
10. IMPLEMENTATION_RULES.md

## Vocabulary policy
The names in this layer are canonical implementation identifiers. Content-facing labels may differ through localization or presentation.

| Implementation term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Vitality | Primary defeat/health meter |
| Strain | Secondary pressure meter that can enable alternate states and outcomes |
| Command | Tactical control resource applied to targets |
| Resolve | Voluntary-risk or vulnerability resource used by reactive builds |
| Pressure | Resource generated by exploiting weaknesses and unstable states |
| Fortitude | Resource generated by enduring damage or resisting pressure |
| Break | A prepared state that reduces resistance and enables special outcomes |
| Overflow | Excess secondary-meter state beyond its normal threshold |
| Aspect | A tagged target domain used by skills and modifiers |

Do not infer presentation, narrative, genre, or theme from these identifiers. Treat them only as mechanical vocabulary.

## Completeness rule
When two documents appear to overlap, resolve conflicts by preferring: explicit lifecycle contract -> explicit integration contract -> system-specific rule -> core model. Do not invent a parallel subsystem to resolve an ambiguity; add or extend a contract using the existing architecture.

## Skill audit and reconciliation status

# AHTW — Skills Audit & Reconciliation

> Status: **SEMANTIC DESIGN CLOSED; NORMALIZED CATALOG BASELINE CREATED; RUNTIME INTEGRATION AND BALANCE QA PENDING**. The semantic baseline is recorded in `docs/systems/combat/SKILL_CATALOG_CONTENT_LOCK.md`; the production candidate and handoff are documented in `docs/systems/combat/SKILL_CATALOG_PRODUCTION_HANDOFF.md`.

## 1. Purpose and audit method

The skill system should create recognizable gameplay loops rather than a collection of isolated actions. Builds should generate and spend resources, exploit tagged states, react to enemy intent, manage risk, and pursue different encounter outcomes.

Audit order:
1. Canonicalize mechanical vocabulary.
2. Define tier semantics and unlock rules.
3. Reconcile legacy entries and the 14 previously approved additions by functional cluster.
4. Give every retained skill a stable ID and structured mechanics.
5. Validate coverage, redundancy, progression, balance, and encounter outcomes.
6. Commit the finalized CSV and companion data only after proposed dispositions are reviewed.

## 2. Approved decisions

### 2.1 Canonical vocabulary and meter migration

The implementation identifiers in `implementation_spec/README.md` and `implementation_spec/COMBAT.md` are the sole mechanical source of truth. Presentation names may be thematic or localized, but must not introduce parallel mechanical resources.

| Canonical identifier | Mechanical meaning | Legacy terminology policy |
|---|---|---|
| Vitality | Primary defeat/health meter | Use for the primary defeat meter; presentation text may say health. |
| Strain | Secondary pressure meter; thresholds and alternate outcomes | Map legacy H-gauge to Strain; validate the meaning of every effect before converting. |
| Command | Tactical control resource | Replace legacy Control when it means a spendable tactical resource. |
| Resolve | Resource generated through deliberate exposure/risk for reactive builds | Legacy Surrender is not a synonym. Surrender is an outcome/state where defined. |
| Pressure | Resource generated by exploiting weaknesses or unstable states | Replace Cruelty only where it means exploitable-state advantage. |
| Fortitude | Resource generated by enduring damage/resisting pressure | Replace Endurance when it means the defensive/reactive resource. |
| Break | Prepared state that reduces resistance and enables special outcomes | Do not use as a generic synonym for stun, disable, or capture. |
| Overflow | Excess Strain beyond its normal threshold | Use the shared Overflow event/contract; do not treat it as a second meter unless separately specified. |
| Aspect | Tagged target domain used by skills and modifiers | Body-part targeting may be represented through Aspect tags where appropriate; validate mappings in content data. |

**Approved migration rule:** legacy `H-gauge` maps to canonical `Strain`; legacy `Health Damage` maps to damage to `Vitality`. This is a terminology rule, not permission to blindly copy old effects. Validate target, direction, timing, scaling, resistance/mitigation, trigger order, duration, and encounter-outcome behavior for each row. Where one legacy description combines several mechanics, split it into ordered structured effects.

Other canonical shared keywords: **Exploit, Consume, Chain, Threshold, Overflow**. These are mechanics/keywords, not resources.

### 2.2 Tier semantics and unlock contract

Tiers express **both progression expectation and expected power**, not raw power alone.

| Tier | Expected progression | Power/design expectation |
|---|---|---|
| Tier 1 | Early access; low or modest prerequisites | Reliable foundation, focused effect, limited setup and manageable opportunity cost. |
| Tier 2 | Requires Skill Points plus meaningful prerequisites; may require a milestone or content gate | Stronger efficiency, specialization, synergy, or flexibility; meaningful condition/cost should justify it. |
| Tier 3 | Significant investment, prerequisite chain, or later progression gate | Build-defining impact, major resource conversion, exceptional utility, or high risk/reward. |

Unlock evaluation combines Skill Points, declarative prerequisites, and progression gates. Use the existing contracts in `implementation_spec/CONTENT_CONTRACTS.md` and `implementation_spec/PROGRESSION_AND_GATING.md`. Avoid arbitrary level-only rules and hidden mandatory requirements. A higher tier is not guaranteed to dominate every lower-tier skill in every context.

### 2.3 Catalog source of truth

- `data/skills/skills.csv` remains the **primary concrete skill catalog**.
- The CSV must gain stable IDs and normalized, machine-readable mechanical fields.
- `implementation_spec/SKILL_CATALOG.md` defines the schema and shared primitives.
- Companion files may define complex effect sequences, formulas, requirement graphs, presentation references, or reusable definitions.
- Natural-language descriptions are not runtime source-of-truth.
- Stable IDs are immutable identity; presentation names can change.
- The semantic reconciliation has been approved. The normalized catalog is authored on `production/normalized-skill-catalog`; the default branch is not changed by this work.

### 2.4 Legacy content and consolidation policy

Preserve the fantasy and player-facing intention where useful, but reformulate, merge, or remove mechanically redundant entries. **Consolidation is by functional cluster, and a merge is justified only when player decision and mechanical result are genuinely redundant.** Similar theme alone is not sufficient reason to remove a skill. New generic skills should not be added unless a demonstrated gap remains after reconciliation and testing.

### 2.5 Capability/Transformation policy

`P-growth` is modeled as a **Capability/Transformation** candidate that grants a stable capability tag and enables dependent skills. It is not an ordinary temporary buff by default. The final record must define target scope, acquisition/unlock, persistence, removal policy, save/load behavior, and all dependent skill references. The current dependency candidates are Autof, F-fk, Penetrate, and A-penetration. The legacy row's Ally targeting conflicts with its described role and must be explicitly resolved before migration.

## 3. Current inventory and confidence

- Legacy inventory: **54 named skill rows** plus **14 approved additions**.
- Normalized candidate: **67 canonical mechanical records** after merging Enslave into Absolute Control.
- The nine unnamed placeholder rows have been removed.
- Stable IDs and normalized references are present in the CSV; nested mechanics live in `data/skills/skill_definitions.json`.
- Numeric values are explicitly provisional; the catalog is not yet playtest-balanced.

## 4. Approved additions

The fourteen proposed neutral IDs are documented in `implementation_spec/SKILL_CATALOG.md` and reconciliation Pass 1/Pass 2. They remain proposed content records until their mechanics and overlaps are approved. Display names may remain thematic/localized.

## 5. Reconciliation documents

- `docs/systems/combat/SKILL_CATALOG_RECONCILIATION_PASS_1.md`: first-pass row-by-row inventory and hypotheses.
- `docs/systems/combat/SKILL_CATALOG_RECONCILIATION_PASS_2.md`: applies approved meter migration, P-growth capability, and cluster-based consolidation policy to all 54 legacy rows and fourteen proposed additions. Proposed dispositions are not deletion orders.

## 6. Requirements for final content lock

Every retained skill needs:
- stable ID and display name;
- kind (Active, Passive, Capability/Transformation, or another registered kind);
- tier and Skill Point cost;
- declarative prerequisites and optional milestone gate;
- target query, actor/target scope, and valid target conditions;
- canonical resource costs/generation;
- structured effects and conditions;
- duration, tick timing, stack/refresh policy, cooldown, Chain/Threshold/Overflow references where applicable;
- encounter-outcome interactions;
- Aspect/state tags;
- presentation reference;
- testable acceptance condition.

Tier is reviewed against impact, reliability, flexibility, setup, cost, and availability—not just damage.

## 7. Production tasks after semantic lock

1. Migrate legacy rows to stable IDs and the normalized schema; remove the nine unnamed placeholder rows.
2. Consolidate Enslave and Absolute Control into one canonical record while preserving presentation aliasing.
3. Add structured companion definitions for effects, targets, scaling, requirements, costs, Chain/Threshold/Overflow, capability dependencies, and presentation.
4. Assign numeric coefficients, costs, durations, cooldowns, caps, and Skill Point costs during balance work.
5. Validate IDs, tiers/kinds, requirement/effect/target references, tags, resource expressions, and encounter-outcome consistency.
6. Run deterministic encounter tests for DoTs, partial multi-target outcomes, Overflow, Chain, high-risk friendly fire, and P-growth gating.

## 8. Current verdict

**Skill architecture and semantic design: CLOSED. Production catalog: NOT YET READY.** The accepted semantic dispositions for all 54 named legacy rows and fourteen proposed additions are recorded in `docs/systems/combat/SKILL_CATALOG_CONTENT_LOCK.md`; Passes 1–7 provide the audit trail. The remaining work is runtime integration, CI enforcement, encounter simulation, numerical tuning, and persistence/UI/localization verification. No additional generic skills are justified at this stage. The normalized catalog is available on the `production/normalized-skill-catalog` branch.


## Canonical semantic/content lock

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Content Lock
## Final semantic dispositions and production handoff

> **Status:** **DESIGN/SEMANTIC RECONCILIATION CLOSED** under the project owner's standing instruction to accept recommendations from this point forward. This lock settles skill identity, effect roles, target/outcome contracts, and consolidation policy. It does not claim numerical balance, runtime implementation, or QA are complete. The legacy `data/skills/skills.csv` is intentionally not migrated in this design-lock commit.

## 1. Canonical system rules

- Vitality is the primary defeat meter; Strain is the pressure/alternate-outcome meter.
- Command is a tactical control resource; Resolve supports deliberate exposure and reactions; Pressure rewards exploiting weaknesses; Fortitude supports defense and retaliation.
- Break is a prepared state, not a synonym for stun, disable, capture, or defeat.
- H-gauge maps semantically to Strain; Health Damage maps to Vitality damage. No blind text substitution.
- Tiers express progression expectation and expected power. Unlocks combine Skill Points, prerequisites, and optional milestone gates.
- Every skill uses a stable ID, explicit actor/target scope, canonical costs/generation, declared effects, scaling profile, requirements, timing, stack/refresh rules, outcome interactions, and presentation reference.
- Primary attributes are weighted first; the matching body-part affinity is then applied as a separate modifier. Multi-Aspect skills must explicitly define their combination rule.
- Similar actions can share effect templates while remaining distinct records when affinity/scaling, requirements, tactical role, cost, or risk changes player decisions.
- No finisher, passive, or reaction may bypass the encounter outcome contract or force global victory.
- Deterministic ordering, explicit caps, and recursion guards are required for multi-target actions, reactions, Chain, and Overflow.
- P-growth grants `CAPABILITY_P_GROWTH` to Alya only. Dependent skills require that capability at action-validation time.
- CSV migration remains a separate production operation; no schema rewrite is performed by this design lock.

## 2. Final legacy dispositions

All 54 named legacy rows have been reviewed. The nine unnamed placeholder rows are not skills and must be removed during eventual migration.

| Legacy entry | Final semantic disposition |
|---|---|
| Sensory Enhancement | Retain as a tagged vulnerability amplifier for explicitly named channels/effects. |
| Mstrbate | Retain as one coherent risk/reward action: reduce Alya's Strain and increase the target's likelihood of choosing a Strain-oriented attack for a declared duration. The risk is explicit and bounded; do not split it into duplicate records. |
| Dancing moves | Retain as an active, time-bounded evasion stance. |
| Flirt | Retain as a hybrid low-to-moderate Strain action and modest Command generator; combined efficiency is below specialized actions. |
| Hypnotize | Retain as intent manipulation or a defined resisted mental-control state; no implicit stun/DoT bundle. |
| Get Spanked | Retain as voluntary exposure that grants Resolve or a bounded reactive payoff when its declared incoming-event trigger resolves. |
| Sensory Deprivation | Retain as a debuff to explicitly named accuracy/evasion/defense stats. |
| Spank | Retain as a focused Strain attack; any Vitality damage is a conditional rider only. |
| Instill protectiveness | Retain as an explicit target-preference/ally-protection effect with named beneficiary and duration. |
| Bl-job | Retain as a basic focused Strain action with its own Aspect/scaling profile. |
| Lay | Retain as a focused Strain action; self-risk is absent unless authored as an explicit trade-off with payoff. |
| Grope | Retain as a focused Strain action with its declared multi-Aspect scaling rule. |
| Service | Retain as a support action with a declared recovery channel; default to Vitality recovery, not ambiguous “healing.” |
| H-job | Retain as a Mind-Aspect Strain DoT against mental/sensory vulnerability; use standard DoT lifecycle. |
| Bend | Retain as the baseline single-target Strain DoT. |
| AS | Retain as a Chain/sequence skill. Advance only when its primary effect applies to at least one valid target; reset on non-chain action, invalid action, encounter end, or loss of required capability/state. |
| S Symbol | Retain as a passive recovery listener on the canonical Overflow event. |
| Otherworldy Charm | Retain as a passive enemy-intent bias; do not duplicate Hypnotize's active control state. |
| Piercing Stares | Retain as a conditional passive Strain effect requiring its declared equipment condition. |
| Sadism | Retain as a passive modifier to Vitality damage dealt, with explicit stacking and cap. |
| Masochism | Retain as a passive modifier to declared mitigation/resistance channels, with explicit stacking and cap. |
| Moans | Retain as a bounded reactive reflection of qualifying incoming Strain; it is not a generic second Counter. |
| Encourage | Retain as an active, time-bounded buff to an ally's next qualifying attack. |
| Flaunt | Retain as reliable area Strain pressure without Strip's evasion rider or G-bang's group-size bonus. |
| Autof | Retain as a P-growth-gated self-recovery/cleanse action; declare each removable effect category. |
| Aphrodisiac | Retain as a single-target Strain DoT plus a bounded increase in the target's likelihood of choosing Strain-oriented attacks; both effects share one duration and are resisted/removed under their declared effect rules. |
| P-growth | Retain as Alya-only Capability/Transformation; declare persistence, revocation, and dependent skills. |
| Facesit | Retain as a focused Strain action against one enemy. The baseline has no self-damage; any future variant that introduces self-risk must declare its payoff and target-side semantics separately. |
| Bondage | Retain as a self-applied restraint state that reduces the user's evasion while increasing the next qualifying Strain action's effectiveness; duration and payoff are explicit, with deterministic expiry. |
| Bully | Retain as a named-stat debuff; do not use generic “weaken” semantics. |
| Bite | Retain as a focused Vitality attack; any Strain effect is conditional only. |
| Bind | Retain as a restraint/control state with explicit blocked actions, resistance, duration, and removal rules. |
| Prolonged Pl- | Retain as a temporary Overflow lock. On expiry/removal, re-evaluate Strain and emit the normal Overflow event if the boundary is met. |
| F-fk | Retain as a P-growth-gated focused Strain action with a capped bonus based on the target's remaining Vitality; declare calculation order. |
| Paizuri | Retain as a basic focused Strain action with its own Aspect/scaling profile. |
| Summon c-s | Retain as an area action with a capped concentration curve: fewer valid enemies increases per-target output, total output remains capped, target count is snapshotted at validation, and zero valid targets invalidates the action without cost. |
| Swallow | Retain as a single-target finisher request gated by Strain threshold/state and the encounter's permitted outcomes. |
| Extra tips | Retain as a passive reward modifier evaluated at encounter reward resolution, with a cap and no duplicate reward roll. |
| Cuteness | Retain as a passive ally attack modifier, with explicit scope and stacking. |
| Naughty Thoughts | Retain as a self-passive Strain accuracy/effectiveness modifier, with explicit cap. |
| C-drinker | Retain as a passive recovery trigger after a qualifying enemy defeat caused by an eligible skill; define recovery channel and event timing. |
| Strip | Retain as area Strain pressure plus a temporary self-evasion window against declared incoming attacks. |
| Pink mist | Retain as area Strain DoT plus a named defense debuff; use standard DoT and stat-debuff lifecycles. |
| Penetrate | Retain as a P-growth-gated focused Strain action; no implied stun. |
| A-penetration | Retain as a P-growth-gated focused Strain action with a defined control rider, subject to resistance and duration. |
| Stomp | Retain as a threshold conversion: Strain state increases Vitality damage under an explicit cap. |
| Summon Tentacles | Retain as a HighRisk area action that may affect enemies, allies, and the user. The default primary effect is Strain damage to every side included in the validation-time target snapshot; any side-specific reduction/rider must be explicitly declared. Resolve targets in deterministic order. |
| Enslave | Merge into the canonical `SKILL_ABSOLUTE_CONTROL` identity. Keep Enslave only as a presentation alias/legacy label, not a duplicate mechanical record. |
| G-bang | Retain as area Strain pressure with a capped bonus that increases with the number of valid enemies; distinct from Flaunt and Summon c-s. |
| Bkakke | Retain as an all-target finisher request. Resolve each target independently; successful outcomes persist. Each failed/ineligible target deals a fixed, separately balanced Strain penalty to Alya; this penalty may trigger Overflow but cannot directly declare encounter defeat. Victory requires every target request to succeed. |
| Deepth | Retain as a basic focused Strain action with its own Aspect/scaling profile. |
| Worship C | Retain as an area intent-bias action; do not also apply an unrelated vulnerability bundle. |
| Ecstasy Aftershocks | Retain as a passive area retaliation listener on the canonical Overflow event. |
| Divine Health | Rework as conditional resistance and/or a restricted cleanse tied to a declared recovery event; no blanket debuff immunity. |

## 3. Fourteen proposed additions — final disposition

| Canonical ID | Display role | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| `SKILL_PROVOKE` | Provoke | Retain: changes enemy target preference/intent toward a declared actor. |
| `SKILL_ENDURE` | Endure | Retain: active mitigation and bounded Fortitude generation. |
| `SKILL_COUNTER` | Counter | Retain: deterministic response to a qualifying declared enemy action/intent. |
| `SKILL_COMMAND` | Command | Retain as a Command-resource generator; it does not also spend Command. |
| `SKILL_RECOVER_COMPOSURE` | Recover Composure | Retain: reduce Strain or remove a narrowly declared eligible state; channel is explicit. |
| `SKILL_EXPLOIT_WEAKNESS` | Exploit Weakness | Retain: consumes/exploits a tagged vulnerability to generate Pressure or amplify a declared channel. |
| `SKILL_BREAK_RESISTANCE` | Break Resistance | Retain as setup that lowers resistance/prepares Break; not a full disable. |
| `SKILL_TURN_THE_TABLES` | Turn the Tables | Retain: converts a specific recent combat event into a bounded response/recovery/retaliation. |
| `SKILL_FOCUS_SURGE` | Focus Surge | Retain: bounded self-buff to the next qualifying action; explicit expiry and no stacking by default. |
| `SKILL_DIRECT_ALLY` | Direct Ally | Retain: improves or redirects one ally action only if action-economy rules permit it. |
| `SKILL_ABSOLUTE_CONTROL` | Absolute Control | Canonical merged Enslave identity: high-investment finisher requesting one authored permitted outcome (Yield, Disable, or Capture). |
| `SKILL_UNBROKEN` | Unbroken | Retain: narrowly prevents one declared defeat/disable event with strict frequency/cooldown limits; never grants general immunity. |
| `SKILL_OVERFLOW_ENGINE` | Overflow Engine | Retain: active/configurable conversion of a canonical Overflow event into one declared output. |
| `SKILL_MUTUAL_RUIN` | Mutual Ruin | Retain as a HighRisk mutual-outcome action with explicit eligibility, both-side consequences, caps, and encounter-outcome validation. |

## 4. Shared rules for the three Overflow skills

- A canonical Overflow event is emitted at most once per affected actor per resolved action when the boundary is crossed.
- S Symbol recovers; Ecstasy Aftershocks retaliates against the declared area; Overflow Engine converts Overflow through a player-configured output.
- Listener order is deterministic. Each listener declares an output cap and cannot recursively trigger itself. A global cross-listener chain-depth cap is required.
- One listener does not consume or suppress the event for other listeners unless its own definition explicitly says so.

## 5. Remaining production work (not unresolved semantic design)

The design/reconciliation section is closed. The following tasks remain for production and must not be mislabeled as open design decisions:

1. **Numeric balance:** coefficients, base powers, resource costs/yields, Skill Point costs, cooldowns, durations, resistance curves, caps, and rounding.
2. **Schema migration:** add stable IDs and normalized logical fields; remove nine blank placeholder rows; represent the Enslave/Absolute Control merge as one record with a presentation alias.
3. **Companion data:** author deterministic target queries, effect sequences, scaling profiles, requirements, Chain/Threshold/Overflow rules, capability dependencies, and presentation references.
4. **Validation:** schema checks for IDs, fields, references, tags, and formulas; encounter-level tests for multi-target resolution, partial finisher success, DoT timing, Overflow listeners, Chain reset, and P-growth gating.
5. **Balance QA:** test single-target, group, high-risk, support, control, recovery, and resource-loop builds. Verify Flirt cannot dominate both dedicated Command generation and dedicated Strain damage.
6. **Documentation synchronization:** after migration, update the implementation spec and project roadmap to distinguish design lock from implementation readiness.

## 6. Exit criteria

The **skill design section** is complete when this lock and Passes 1–7 are treated as the approved semantic baseline. The implementation catalog is complete only after the production work above is finished and validated. This distinction avoids claiming an unbalanced or untested CSV is production-ready.


## Scaling audit

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Scaling Audit

> **Status:** Proposed content-lock audit for 54 named legacy skills and 14 proposed additions. This does not modify the CSV, approve numeric coefficients, or silently finalize conditional merges.

## 1. Approved design decision

The project owner confirmed that the body part used by a skill can influence effectiveness through attributes associated with that specific skill. Similar-looking actions may therefore remain separate when their scaling profile, target requirements, risks, triggers, resource loops, synergies, or progression choices produce meaningful build decisions.

This audit proposes per-skill attributes, canonical effects, synergies, and distinct identity. These are recommendations, not locked balance values. Exact coefficients, caps, durations, costs, and unlock requirements remain open.

## 2. Attribute vocabulary

The eight primary attributes are:

| Code | Attribute | Existing character-sheet roles |
|---|---|---|
| BODY | Body | Vitality/HP capacity, physical damage and physical damage resistance |
| AGI | Agility | Evasion, accuracy, initiative |
| SOUL | Soul | Magical attack, Strain resistance |
| CHARM | Charm | Strain damage/accuracy and likelihood of eliciting enemy Strain-oriented attacks |
| DOM | Dominance | Strain damage/accuracy and resistance to Strain-oriented attacks |
| SUB | Submission | Enemy Strain-oriented attack likelihood, Strain damage and buff empowerment |
| SAD | Sadism | Physical/Vitality damage and accuracy |
| MAS | Masochism | Strain resistance, physical damage resistance and debuff resistance |

Existing body-part multipliers/affinities on Alya's sheet are Ass, Breasts, Crotch, Mouth, and Mind. Treat these as a distinct per-skill scaling input, not extra primary attributes. Their exact formulas are not yet defined.

Each skill's scaling profile should declare: (1) primary and secondary attributes; (2) relevant body-part affinity; (3) whether the action is physical, magical, Strain-focused, Vitality-focused, support, or mixed; (4) contextual/target-state modifiers; and (5) any cap or safety rule. Attribute-to-body-part mapping is **per skill**, not universal. An Aspect tag or requirement alone does not define scaling.

Conceptually, effectiveness may combine declared attribute contribution, declared body-part affinity, and context modifiers. The implementation must use the project's canonical modifier pipeline; do not automatically multiply every term together. For All-body-part actions, explicitly choose a relevant affinity, a defined composite, or no part multiplier; never silently multiply every affinity together.

## 3. Legacy skill audit — 54 named entries

In each row, the first attributes are suggested leading contributors, not approved weights. Part refers to the existing body-part affinity. Effect and synergy are proposed identities; final values remain open.

### A. Control, charm, intent and mental pressure

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory Enhancement | SOUL / CHARM; Mind | Apply a tagged vulnerability to a named channel: Strain, Vitality, or a specific action tag | Enables Exploit/Pressure and follow-up actions. Amplifies a named channel rather than generally weakening the enemy. |
| Flirt | CHARM / DOM; Mouth | Generate Command and deal Strain damage; balance both outputs against action cost | Hybrid pressure/resource action feeds the Command → Break → Control loop while contributing direct pressure. Prevent it from outperforming dedicated generators or attacks in both roles. |
| Hypnotize | SOUL + DOM / CHARM; Mind | Change locked enemy intent or apply a defined mental-control state with resistance, eligibility and duration | Combines with Command/Break. Changes enemy options, unlike a damage-only action. |
| Sensory Deprivation | SOUL / DOM; Mind | Debuff named accuracy/evasion/defense stats with duration and stacking rules | Supports evasion/counter and risky follow-ups. Impairs performance rather than amplifying a damage channel. |
| Otherworldy Charm | CHARM / DOM; Mind or declared composite | Conditional/persistent bias toward eligible Strain-oriented actions and away from Vitality attacks | Supports Strain/exposure builds. Keep distinct from Hypnotize only if passive/persistent scope differs from active control. |
| Aphrodisiac | SOUL + CHARM / DOM; Mind | Strain-over-time OR enemy-intent modifier; combine only if a coherent state | Supports DoT or intent builds. Must differ from Otherworldly Charm and generic DoT by scope/trigger/state. |
| Bully | CHARM + SAD / DOM; Mouth | Intimidation debuff to named offensive stats, such as attack effectiveness and accuracy/defense | Sets up damage/control. Weakens capability rather than directly changing intent. |
| Worship C | SUB + CHARM / DOM; Breasts/Mouth | Area intent bias OR a named vulnerability/debuff; choose one primary effect | Supports group Strain strategy. Keep if area scope or ritual setup makes it distinct from Otherworldly Charm/Aphrodisiac. |

### B. Exposure, defense, reactions and protection

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dancing moves | AGI / MAS; All/composite | Active evasion stance for a declared window and attack set | Pairs with Provoke and visible intent. Proactive avoidance, not mitigation after a hit. |
| Get Spanked | MAS + SUB / BODY; Ass | Deliberate exposure; when a qualifying incoming trigger resolves, generate Resolve or another bounded payoff | Builds Resolve for Counter/Turn the Tables. Risk-for-resource choice, not just intent redirection. |
| Instill protectiveness | SUB + CHARM / MAS; All/composite | Explicit ally protection, guard priority, or target redirection; name beneficiary and exposed actor | Synergy with Guard/interception and Provoke. Legacy wording is ambiguous and must not be copied literally. |
| Spank | BODY + SAD / AGI; Ass | One primary damage channel; any second channel is an explicit conditional rider | Ass affinity and BODY/SAD scaling create a build choice. Define the primary channel and order any conditional rider. |
| Bite | BODY + SAD / AGI; Mouth | One primary damage channel; any second channel is an explicit conditional rider | Mouth affinity creates a different scaling route from Spank. Define the primary channel and order any conditional rider. |
| Masochism | MAS / SUB; Ass/Breasts | BuildPassive giving bounded mitigation/Fortitude benefit under a qualifying exposure/damage trigger | Synergizes with Endure/Get Spanked. Must not duplicate active mitigation or create unlimited passive reduction. |
| Moans | MAS + SUB / CHARM; Mouth | Reflect a bounded portion of qualifying incoming Strain after the original event resolves | Exposure build synergy. Distinct from Counter if automatic reflection differs from an active response. |
| Bondage | MAS + SOUL / SUB; All/composite | Self-restraint stance trading a declared limitation for defense, resistance, or a bounded area effect | Defensive/HighRisk builds. Current wording lacks causal clarity; the trade-off must be real and deterministic. |

### C. Basic physical/Strain actions and sustained effects

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mstrbate | MAS + CHARM / SOUL; Crotch | Split self-recovery from enemy-intent influence unless a coherent risk/reward loop joins them | Can support self-sustain or intent builds. Retain both effects only if the action creates a testable trade-off. |
| Bl-job | CHARM + SUB / SOUL; Mouth | Focused Strain action using a shared basic-action template | Mouth affinity and SUB/CHARM passives may distinguish it. Separate skill only if scaling, requirement, cost or synergy differs. |
| Lay | SUB + MAS / BODY; Crotch | Focused Strain action; self-risk only when authored as an explicit trade-off | Body-part/attribute scaling may support a build choice. Any self-risk must declare trigger, consequence, and payoff. |
| Grope | CHARM + AGI / SUB; Ass + Breasts | Focused Strain action; multiple-part scaling only if composite is defined | Supports evasive/teasing builds. Distinct if multi-part affinity or a secondary effect changes tactical use. |
| H-job | AGI + CHARM / SUB; Mind Aspect retained | Focused Strain DoT against a declared mental/sensory vulnerability; specify tick, duration, refresh/stack, and resistance rules | Owner decision: DoT identity. Mind must have a real mechanical meaning; do not reinterpret it as a physical body part. |
| Service | SOUL + SUB / CHARM; Mouth | Ally support restoring a named channel (Vitality or Strain reduction) OR removing a whitelisted effect | Recovery/team sustain. Distinct from buffs because it repairs an existing problem; “heal” must specify the operation. |
| Bend | SUB + MAS / CHARM; Ass | Baseline single-target Strain-over-time with explicit tick timing and duration | Establishes the basic DoT and enables vulnerability/Pressure follow-up. |
| AS | SUB + MAS / CHARM; Ass | Chain/sequence mechanic with explicit reset conditions | Owner decision: retain as a Chain skill. Define qualifying consecutive uses, reset events, output progression, and interaction with DoT duration; if no meaningful Chain behavior can be specified, consolidate with Bend. |
| Paizuri | CHARM + SUB / BODY; Breasts | Focused Strain attack on shared basic-action template | Breast affinity/equipment/passive synergies may distinguish it. Identical scaling/effects/costs make it a presentation variant. |

| Facesit | BODY + SUB / MAS; Crotch | Focused action with one primary channel; self-risk only as an explicit trade-off | Legacy Enemy + Self target field is not itself a self-effect. Declare any self-cost separately, or remove Self from the target query. |

### D. Area actions and high-risk effects

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flaunt | CHARM + DOM / AGI; All/composite | Mechanically distinct area Strain pressure action; define a unique targeting/scaling/cost rule | Owner decision: retain as distinct. Proposed role: broad, reliable area pressure without Strip's evasion rider; distinguish it from G-bang with a documented mechanical rule. |
| Summon c-s | SOUL + SUB / CHARM; Mind | Summoned area Strain effect whose per-target output increases as enemy count falls, with curve/floor/cap | Distinct summon that concentrates force against fewer targets. Define target set and friendly-fire policy. |
| Strip | AGI + CHARM / DOM; All/composite | Area Strain plus temporary evasion with defined duration and eligible incoming attacks | Owner decision: retain as distinct. The evasion window is its defining tactical identity and must resolve in a fixed order relative to the area effect. |
| Pink mist | SOUL + DOM / CHARM; Mind | Area Strain-over-time plus one named defense debuff, with duration/ticks/stacking | Persistent area pressure and debuff synergy; distinct from direct AoE because value persists and may be cleansed/resisted. |
| Summon Tentacles | SOUL + DOM + MAS / SUB; Mind/All composite | HighRisk area action may affect enemies, allies, and the user; each side/effect is declared in the skill data | Owner decision: all sides may be affected. Exact side/effect matrix and consequences remain to be specified; do not use vague “aggro everyone.” |
| G-bang | SUB + CHARM / SOUL; All/composite | Mechanically distinct area Strain attack with declared target set and a unique scaling rule | Owner decision: retain as distinct. Proposed role: area pressure that scales upward with the number of valid enemies, with explicit cap; distinguish from Summon c-s, which concentrates output against fewer enemies. |

### E. Threshold conversion and finishers

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Prolonged Pl- | DOM + SUB / SOUL; Crotch | Strain threshold/Overflow interaction; decide whether it prevents Overflow, locks a threshold, or converts follow-up attacks into Vitality damage | Threshold build synergy. Current wording is contradictory; choose one deterministic state transition. |
| F-fk | BODY + DOM / SAD; Crotch | P-growth-gated action scaling against target below a declared Vitality threshold, with cap and explicit target check | Capability + execute pressure. Distinct from Penetrate if low-Vitality scaling creates a tactical window, not just a larger number. |
| Penetrate | BODY + DOM / SAD; Crotch | P-growth-gated focused Strain attack | Baseline capability-gated action. Distinct from A-penetration if the latter's control/Break rider matters. |

| Bkakke | SUB + MAS / SOUL; Mouth + Breasts composite | All-target finisher request; victory only if every enemy is eligible and every request succeeds; each failed/ineligible target triggers a declared self-risk consequence | Owner decision: successful target outcomes persist; failed targets cause self-risk; the encounter continues if any target fails. Exact risk channel/magnitude remains open. |
| Enslave / Absolute Control | DOM + SOUL / CHARM; Mind | One shared top-tier finisher identity with canonical eligibility, resistance, duration, permitted outcome and failure behavior | Owner decision: merge Enslave and Absolute Control. Legacy names may be presentation variants, not duplicate mechanical entries. |

### F. Passives, Overflow, capability and team support

| Skill | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| S Symbol | MAS + SOUL / SUB; All/composite | Passive recovery on one defined Overflow event: choose Vitality recovery, Strain reduction or a specific cleanse | Overflow survival build. Must differ from Ecstasy Aftershocks offense and Overflow Engine active conversion. |
| Piercing Stares | CHARM + DOM / SOUL; Breasts | ConditionalPassive triggered by equipment carrying a registered piercing tag; apply declared Strain effect | Equipment-driven identity. Use equipment tags, never hard-coded item IDs. |
| Sadism | SAD / BODY; Crotch/Mouth as action profile requires | BuildPassive improving a named Vitality-damage/exploitation payoff under a qualifying trigger | Synergizes with Pressure/vulnerability and physical attacks. Bounded and conditional, not an uncapped global multiplier. |
| Encourage | CHARM + SUB / SOUL; legacy Ass tag needs validation | Active ally buff for a declared next action or short duration; name affected stat/channel | Ally burst/coordinated turns. Distinct from Cuteness if active timing/action cost differs from passive uptime. |
| Autof | BODY + MAS / SOUL; Breast/Crotch/Mouth per action profile | P-growth-gated self-recovery plus cleanse whitelist; specify Vitality, Strain and removable tags | Capability build. Distinct if the gate and multi-channel recovery create a real commitment. |
| P-growth | SOUL + BODY / DOM; Crotch + Mind as currently listed | Capability/Transformation granting CAPABILITY_P_GROWTH to Alya only, with explicit persistence and revocation | Owner decision: Alya-only. Unlocks dependent skills for Alya; never grant this capability to allies. |
| Extra tips | CHARM + SOUL / AGI; legacy Mind tag needs validation | Reward/economy passive on a declared reward event, with cap and stacking policy | Supports economy/exploration, not a combat loop. Move to reward catalog unless passive skills share one unified catalog. |
| Cuteness | CHARM + SUB / SOUL; Breasts + Mouth | Passive ally attack modifier with explicit scope, stat/channel and stacking | Team buff builds. Distinct from Encourage if passive uptime versus active timing is meaningful. |

## 4. Fourteen proposed additions

These are candidates from the current audit, not rows currently present in the CSV.

| Candidate | Suggested scaling | Proposed effect | Synergy / distinct identity |
|---|---|---|---|
| SKILL_PROVOKE — Provoke | CHARM + DOM / SUB | Change enemy intent/target preference toward a declared actor for a bounded window | Enables Guard/interception, Dancing Moves and Endure. Redirects threat without inherently rewarding self-exposure. |
| SKILL_ENDURE — Endure | BODY + MAS / SUB | Active mitigation against a declared incoming event; may generate capped Fortitude | Distinct from Masochism because it spends an action and responds to a known threat. |
| SKILL_COUNTER — Counter | AGI + SAD / MAS | Reaction after a qualifying enemy action, with deterministic trigger window and action budget | Distinct from automatic Moans reflection and event-history Turn the Tables. |
| SKILL_COMMAND — Command | DOM + CHARM / SOUL | Explicitly choose generator or spender role; never leave resource function ambiguous | Supports Hypnotize, Break Resistance and Absolute Control without duplicating direct control. |
| SKILL_RECOVER_COMPOSURE — Recover Composure | SOUL + MAS / CHARM | Recover Strain or remove a narrow whitelist of effects; choose a primary operation | Recovery after pressure/control. Distinct from Autof if it lacks P-growth gate and has a different profile. |
| SKILL_EXPLOIT_WEAKNESS — Exploit Weakness | AGI + SAD / SOUL | Exploit a named vulnerability/unstable state for a conditional payoff and/or generate Pressure | Distinct from Sensory Enhancement: exploit an existing opening rather than applying it. |
| SKILL_BREAK_RESISTANCE — Break Resistance | DOM + SOUL / CHARM | Lower a named resistance or add Break progress; does not itself equal disable | Sets up Hypnotize, Bind and outcome requests. Setup role, not the finisher. |
| SKILL_TURN_THE_TABLES — Turn the Tables | AGI + MAS / SAD | Consume a specific recent event to recover, retaliate or reverse a state | Uses event history/resource conversion, unlike an immediate direct counter. |
| SKILL_FOCUS_SURGE — Focus Surge | SOUL + CHARM / DOM | Temporary focus buff to a named action channel, with duration/cost | Setup turns and caster builds. Distinct from Naughty Thoughts if active commitment differs from passive tag bonus. |
| SKILL_DIRECT_ALLY — Direct Ally | CHARM + DOM / SOUL | Improve ally's next action or grant a tactical action only if action-economy rules permit | Coordinated team turns. Distinct from Encourage if it changes action opportunity/targeting, not just buff magnitude. |
| SKILL_ABSOLUTE_CONTROL — Absolute Control | DOM + SOUL / CHARM | Merged into the shared Enslave/Absolute Control finisher identity | No separate mechanical catalog row unless a future decision establishes a distinct requirement/scaling profile. |
| SKILL_UNBROKEN — Unbroken | BODY + MAS / SAD | Prevent one narrowly defined defeat/disable event, with strict cooldown/once-per-encounter limit | Emergency exception after mitigation fails, not ordinary damage reduction. |
| SKILL_OVERFLOW_ENGINE — Overflow Engine | SOUL + DOM / MAS | Active/configurable conversion of a resolved Overflow event into one declared output, with recursion/output caps | Distinct from automatic recovery or reactive damage only if player choice matters. |
| SKILL_MUTUAL_RUIN — Mutual Ruin | BODY + SAD + MAS / DOM | HighRisk exchange of a declared self-cost for a bounded substantial target effect | Synergizes with Resolve, Fortitude and Unbroken. Retain only if risk/payoff creates a real decision; never bypass encounter outcomes. |

## 5. Cross-skill synergy map

| Build pattern | Core pieces | Tactical identity |
|---|---|---|
| Command → Break → Outcome | Flirt or Command → Break Resistance → Hypnotize/Enslave/Absolute Control | Invest actions/resources to shape intent, lower resistance, then request a legal outcome. |
| Exposure → Resolve → Reaction | Get Spanked → Resolve → Counter/Turn the Tables; Endure/Masochism for survival | Accept or mitigate known risk to gain a reactive advantage. |
| Evasion and threat control | Provoke → Dancing Moves → Counter | Choose who is threatened, evade the declared attack, punish a qualifying action. |
| Vulnerability → Exploit → Conversion | Sensory Enhancement → Exploit Weakness/Pressure → Stomp or finisher | Create an opening, exploit it, convert Strain into Vitality progress or a permitted outcome. |
| DoT/area pressure | Bend/AS/Pink Mist → vulnerability/debuff → threshold action | Persistent pressure while preparing a threshold payoff. |
| Overflow | Strain buildup → Overflow → S Symbol or Ecstasy Aftershocks or Overflow Engine | Build around recovery, automatic retaliation, or deliberate conversion. |
| Capability specialization | P-growth → Autof/Penetrate/A-penetration/F-fk candidates | Capability changes the available action space. |
| Active/passive team support | Encourage/Direct Ally vs Cuteness/Divine Health | Choose active timing/control or passive-slot investment. |
| Body-part affinity specialization | Distinct scaling profiles plus relevant equipment/passive tags | Similar effects remain separate when their attributes/affinities support different builds. |

## 6. Decisions still open

1. Enslave vs Absolute Control: keep both only if Strain-threshold/state conversion and Command/Break investment create different setup loops or outcomes.
2. Basic action family: keep separate unlockable skills when scaling, requirements, costs, risk, hooks or synergies differ; otherwise use presentation variants over shared effects.
3. AoE: Flaunt/G-bang/Strip may share a template; Summon c-s and Summon Tentacles can differ through target-count scaling and friendly-fire risk.
4. Overflow: recovery, reactive AoE and active conversion should remain separate only if they create different player choices.
5. P-growth target scope, persistence and revocation remain unresolved.
6. The exact relationship between body-part multipliers and attribute contributions remains open; this audit proposes candidates, not formulas.
7. H-job, Encourage, Extra tips and C-drinker have legacy Aspect/part fields that require validation against the intended action.

## 7. Required production pass

Every retained record needs stable ID, display name, kind, tier, cost, requirements, target query, scaling_ref, resource costs/generation, ordered effects/conditions, durations/tick/stack/expiry rules, cooldown, Chain/Threshold/Overflow references, encounter-outcome interactions, presentation reference, and an acceptance test.

**No numeric coefficients are approved here.** Balance should begin after scaling profiles, effect semantics and overlap decisions are explicit.


## Historical reconciliation pass 1 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation, Pass 1

> First-pass recommendations for review. This document does not silently delete legacy content or assert that proposed changes are already implemented. Final dispositions must be approved before the CSV migration.

## 1. Inventory correction

A fresh line-level check of `data/skills/skills.csv` found **54 named legacy skill rows** and **9 unnamed placeholder rows**. The prior planning estimate of 53 named rows was off by one; this correction supersedes the earlier estimate. Adding the 14 approved candidates gives **68 theoretical entries before reconciliation**, not 67.

The placeholder rows are not skills and should be removed during the actual CSV migration. Do not delete or rewrite legacy records until their dispositions are reviewed.

## 2. Legacy row-by-row recommendations

Disposition vocabulary:
- **Keep / normalize:** distinct role appears worth preserving; clean name/ID and specify mechanics.
- **Rework:** preserve the fantasy/intention but replace ambiguous text with canonical mechanics.
- **Conditional:** retain only if testing shows a distinct choice.
- **Merge candidate:** likely redundant with another skill; preserve useful identity through tags, presentation, or a distinct effect.
- **Move out of combat skill catalog:** effect primarily belongs to economy/reward/progression or a capability definition rather than an ordinary combat action.

| Legacy display name | First-pass disposition | Intended distinct role / audit question |
|---|---|---|
| Sensory Enhancement | Rework | Target vulnerability amplifier. Specify whether it modifies Strain, Vitality, or both and use a tagged effect. |
| Mstrbate | Rework / rename | Self-recovery plus deliberate risk is too broad as written. Separate recovery from enemy-behavior manipulation if both are retained. Normalize the typo in presentation. |
| Dancing moves | Keep / normalize | Active evasion or defensive stance. Define duration, whether it affects intent/accuracy, and its action opportunity cost. |
