| Spank | Keep / normalize | Focused physical attack with its own Aspect tag and damage profile; avoid relying on body-part prose alone. |
| Instill protectiveness | Rework / merge candidate | Ally protection or intent redirection. Compare with Provoke and avoid hidden target-priority manipulation. |
| Bl-job | Conditional | Legacy action with a distinct Aspect identity only if it offers a meaningful tactical effect beyond a generic Strain attack. |
| Lay | Conditional | Same audit standard: preserve if its targeting, cost, risk, or effect creates a different decision. |
| Grope | Conditional | Consider merging into a broader multi-Aspect action if it differs only by flavor and damage. |
| Service | Rework | Ally support/recovery. Specify whether it restores Vitality, reduces Strain, or removes effects; do not conflate them. |
| H-job | Conditional | Preserve only if its Mind/Aspect interaction creates a distinct mechanical role. |
| Bend | Merge candidate | Single-target damage-over-time overlaps AS. Preserve one as a basic application and the other only if its chain/escalation rule is distinct. |
| AS | Rework / merge candidate | Repeated-use Chain candidate. Specify reset conditions and whether it increases Strain dealt or vulnerability to it. |
| S Symbol | Rework | Passive recovery on Overflow. Distinguish from Ecstasy Aftershocks and Overflow Engine; define exact trigger and output. |
| Otherworldy Charm | Keep / normalize | Conditional enemy-behavior or attack-channel modifier. Rename display spelling; specify its scope and stacking policy. |
| Piercing Stares | Keep / normalize | ConditionalPassive linked to an equipment tag. The equipment requirement must be data-driven. |
| Sadism | Rework | BuildPassive that increases a declared exploit/Pressure payoff. Avoid a global unbounded Vitality-damage multiplier. |
| Masochism | Rework | BuildPassive for Fortitude/mitigation loop. Avoid a flat global damage reduction that eclipses Endure. |
| Moans | Rework / merge candidate | Reactive reflect effect. Define trigger, cap, damage channel, and relationship to Counter. |
| Encourage | Keep / normalize | Ally support buff. Specify the next-action scope and expiry. |
| Flaunt | Conditional | Area Strain attack. Compare with other AoE attacks and give it a distinct cost, target filter, or secondary effect. |
| Autof | Rework | Recovery/cleanse dependent on P-growth. Define cleanse whitelist and treat the prerequisite as a capability tag. |
| Aphrodisiac | Rework / conditional | Damage-over-time plus enemy intent modifier. Keep both only if they form a coherent shared effect. |
| P-growth | Reclassify candidate | If it changes available skills/targeting/body configuration, model as Capability/Transformation rather than an ordinary buff. Confirm persistence and removal rules. |
| Facesit | Conditional | Clarify the unusual Enemy + Self target declaration. Preserve only with explicit self-risk/target semantics and a distinct tactical payoff. |
| Bondage | Rework | Self-applied defensive/control state or a target restriction. Define who is bound, what changes, and how it ends. |
| Bully | Keep / normalize | Debuff to enemy offensive stats/intent. It can carry multiple tags, but must remain one record despite appearing in multiple archetype lists. |
| Bite | Keep / normalize | Focused physical attack; retain if its conditional effect or Aspect interaction differs from Spank. |
| Bind | Rework / conditional | Disable or restraint with resistance and duration. Differentiate from Hypnotize and Break preparation. |
| Prolonged Pl- | Rework / rename | Its current text appears to block Overflow and trigger damage under a meter condition. Specify Strain/Overflow semantics; do not use a legacy meter name. |
| F-fk | Conditional | P-growth-gated action. Keep only if its low-Vitality scaling creates a distinct risk/reward decision and is numerically capped. |
| Paizuri | Conditional | Retain as a distinct action only if it differs mechanically from other single-target Strain attacks. |
| Summon c-s | Rework / rename | Summon/capability attack. Fix the truncated name; define target set, scaling by enemy count, and whether targets include allies. |
| Swallow | Rework | Single-target finisher candidate. Eligibility should produce a configured encounter outcome rather than directly ending combat outside the shared outcome system. |
| Extra tips | Move out of ordinary combat catalog / conditional passive | Reward bonus is mainly a reward/economy modifier. Keep in the skill catalog only if it is a learnable passive with explicit reward hooks. |
| Cuteness | Rework | Ally buff; define stat/effect, target scope, duration, and stacking. |
| Naughty Thoughts | Rework | Self/build passive that improves a defined accuracy or Strain effect. Avoid vague “accuracy and damage” bonuses. |
| C-drinker | Rework | On-defeat recovery trigger. Define whether the event is defeated by Strain, defeated by this actor, or any encounter defeat; avoid ambiguous event hooks. |
| Strip | Rework / conditional | Currently combines area damage and self-evasion. Decide whether this is a coherent high-tempo action or split the effects. |
| Pink mist | Keep / normalize | Area debuff/damage-over-time. Define target filters, duration, and interaction with equivalent effects. |
| Penetrate | Conditional | P-growth-gated single-target attack. Differentiate from A-penetration by tactical role rather than name alone. |
| A-penetration | Rework / conditional | If retained, make its disable/Break interaction distinct from Penetrate and use standard resistance/outcome rules. |
| Stomp | Keep / normalize | Conditional Vitality damage based on a declared Strain threshold; use Threshold rather than hard-coded prose. |
| Summon Tentacles | Rework | High-risk area effect with possible friendly fire. Define all valid targets, risk mitigation, and enemy-count scaling explicitly. |
| Enslave | Rework / conditional | Control/finisher fantasy. Do not force a global permanent state; map to encounter-defined Disable, Yield, Capture, or another allowed outcome. |
| G-bang | Conditional / merge candidate | Area Strain attack. Compare with Flaunt and Summon Tentacles; retain only if its target/cost/secondary-effect profile is distinct. |
| Bkakke | Rework / conditional | All-target threshold finisher/high-risk outcome. Must use encounter outcome contracts and clearly define what happens to surviving targets. |
| Deepth | Rework / rename | Normalize truncated display name. Keep only if its trigger/Aspect role differs from generic single-target Strain attacks. |
| Worship C | Conditional | Area debuff/behavior modifier. Clarify whether it increases enemy Strain vulnerability, changes intent, or does both. |
| Ecstasy Aftershocks | Keep / normalize | Reactive area effect triggered by Overflow. Distinguish from S Symbol's recovery and Overflow Engine's conversion. |
| Divine Health | Rework | Passive protection/cleanse. Full debuff immunity may invalidate status systems; consider limited resistance or conditional cleanse with explicit caps. |

## 3. Approved additions: first-pass review

All fourteen previously approved additions remain candidates, with canonical mechanical IDs documented in `SKILLS_AUDIT.md` and `implementation_spec/SKILL_CATALOG.md`. They should be reconciled against legacy candidates before adding records. Particular tests:
- **Provoke vs Get Spanked vs Instill protectiveness:** target intent, self-exposure, and ally protection must be distinct.
- **Endure vs Masochism vs Unbroken:** ordinary mitigation, passive build identity, and emergency defeat prevention must not collapse into the same effect.
- **Counter vs Moans vs Turn the Tables:** define trigger windows and whether the response deals damage, reflects damage, or converts a recent event.
- **Command vs BreakResistance vs AbsoluteControl:** resource generation/control, resistance reduction/Break preparation, and encounter outcome should be separate stages.
- **S Symbol vs Ecstasy Aftershocks vs OverflowEngine:** recovery, reactive area output, and deliberate resource conversion must be distinct.
- **ExploitWeakness vs Sensory Enhancement/Otherworldly Charm:** define whether each creates vulnerability, generates Pressure, or merely modifies a specific action.

## 4. Proposed disposition policy

No skill should be removed solely because its theme is similar to another. Merge/remove only when its player decision and mechanical outcome are redundant. A thematic name can survive as a display label, an animation, a VFX profile, a conditional presentation, or a tag after its mechanics are consolidated.

A retained skill must have:
1. one stable ID;
2. one primary tactical role;
3. explicit target/cost/requirements;
4. declared canonical resources and meters;
5. a distinct effect/trigger or a deliberate reason to be a simple baseline action;
6. a tier and unlock path;
7. outcome behavior compatible with the encounter contract;
8. a testable acceptance condition.

## 5. Recommended next pass

Review the table by clusters rather than making 54 independent decisions in isolation. Suggested order:
1. resource/control/reactive loop;
2. finishers and encounter outcomes;
3. Overflow/threshold/Strain effects;
4. passives and capability/transformation;
5. physical/magical attacks and body/Aspect differentiation;
6. rewards/economy-linked skills;
7. tier, costs, prerequisites, and numeric balance.

Only after this pass should the CSV receive stable IDs and structured fields. Then run validation for duplicate IDs, missing references, invalid requirements, malformed rows, and effect/outcome consistency.


## Historical reconciliation pass 2 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation, Pass 2

> Status: **PROPOSED MECHANICAL DISPOSITION — REVIEW REQUIRED**. Pass 2 applies the three newly approved decisions to every legacy row and the fourteen proposed additions. It is more concrete than Pass 1, but it does not authorize deletion or migration of the CSV yet.

## 1. Decisions now locked

1. **Meter migration:** legacy `H-gauge` maps to canonical `Strain`; legacy `Health Damage` maps to damage to `Vitality`. This is a terminology migration rule, not proof that every old effect was described correctly. Validate each skill's actual trigger, target, direction, scaling, and outcome semantics before converting its effect.
2. **P-growth:** model as a `Capability/Transformation` definition that grants a stable capability tag and enables dependent skills. Do not treat it as a temporary generic buff unless a separate, explicit temporary mode is designed. Persistence, removal, target eligibility, and unlock behavior must be declared.
3. **Consolidation:** review by functional cluster. Merge only where the player decision and mechanical result are genuinely redundant; preserve thematic skills when targeting, timing, resource loop, risk, Aspect, or outcome differs in a meaningful way.

## 2. Migration contract for legacy meters

| Legacy wording | Canonical interpretation | Required validation before migration |
|---|---|---|
| H-gauge / H-damage | Strain meter / Strain damage | Determine whether the old value is a meter change, damage-over-time, threshold check, resistance modifier, or trigger. Normalize to the corresponding structured effect, not just a renamed string. |
| Health Damage | Damage to Vitality | Confirm damage timing, mitigation, scaling, target, and whether it is direct damage or an outcome effect. |
| H-gauge overfills / overfill | Strain reaches/exceeds its defined limit and triggers Overflow | Use the canonical Overflow event/contract. Define once-per-event vs repeated triggers, caps, and ordering. |
| H-gauge threshold | Strain Threshold condition | Reference a named threshold predicate; avoid hard-coded prose-only comparisons. |
| Stun / paralysis / forced submission | Not a meter alias | Reconcile as a state, effect, or encounter outcome with eligibility, resistance, duration, and removal rules. |
| Heal | Restore Vitality, reduce Strain, or remove effects—these are separate operations | Each healing skill must name its exact output; never infer that “heal” means all three. |

**Important:** the two meter mappings are canonical vocabulary only. They do not imply that all H-damage should be copied into Strain damage unchanged. The old descriptions may combine meter damage, Vitality damage, states, or outcome behavior; split them into ordered structured effects when needed.

## 3. Capability/Transformation contract for P-growth

Proposed record: `SKILL_P_GROWTH`, kind `Capability` or registered `CapabilityTransformation` kind, with a stable granted capability such as `CAPABILITY_P_GROWTH`.

- Acquisition: a learnable/unlockable capability with Tier 2 expectation, subject to normal Skill Point, prerequisite, and progression-gate rules.
- Effect: grant the capability and make dependent skills eligible; it should not also deal damage, heal, or apply an unrelated temporary combat buff.
- Dependencies: Autof, F-fk, Penetrate, and A-penetration must reference the capability in declarative requirements. Add further dependencies only if the catalog confirms them.
- Persistence/removal: define whether it is permanent after acquisition, removable by a specific narrative/gameplay event, or mutually exclusive with another capability. Do not let ordinary dispel/cleanse remove it by default.
- Targeting: resolve whether the capability is self-only, character configuration, or can be granted to an ally; the legacy row says Ally, so this remains an explicit content question rather than silently assuming Self.
- Safety/consistency: capability grants must be deterministic and save/load persistent if permanent; presentation can remain thematic while the runtime identifier is neutral.

## 4. Pass 2 matrix — 54 named legacy rows

Disposition meanings: **Retain** = keep as a distinct candidate; **Rework** = preserve intent but change/clarify mechanics; **Conditional** = keep only if playtest shows a distinct decision; **Merge candidate** = compare directly with named cluster members before deciding; **Move** = belongs primarily outside ordinary combat resolution. These are proposed dispositions, not final removals.

| Legacy entry | Proposed Pass 2 disposition | Canonical mechanical intent | Decision / acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory Enhancement | Rework | Apply a tagged vulnerability effect that increases a declared Strain or Vitality channel | Must state which channel(s), magnitude, duration, stack policy, and eligible targets; compare with Exploit Weakness. |
| Mstrbate | Rework / rename | Self-recovery with a deliberate-risk or enemy-intent component only if they form one coherent choice | Split into separate skills if recovery and intent manipulation have independent value; normalize display name without making the theme the ID. |
| Dancing moves | Retain | Active evasion/defensive stance | Define duration, evasion/accuracy interaction, stacking, and whether using it consumes the normal action. |
| Flirt | Conditional | Generate Command **or** deal focused Strain; choose one primary loop | Retain separately from Hypnotize only if it has a distinct resource/action-economy role. |

## 5. Pass 2 matrix — fourteen approved additions

These additions remain candidates until their relationship to the legacy rows is resolved. A proposed new skill must not duplicate a legacy effect that can be retained with a stable ID.

| Proposed ID | Proposed role after Pass 2 | Direct comparisons | Proposed disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SKILL_PROVOKE` | Manipulate enemy intent toward a chosen target/self | Get Spanked; Instill protectiveness | Retain if intent redirection is explicit and distinct from Resolve-generating exposure. |
| `SKILL_ENDURE` | Active mitigation plus Fortitude generation | Masochism; Unbroken | Retain as a timed reactive/defensive action, not passive blanket reduction. |
| `SKILL_COUNTER` | Reaction to a declared incoming action/trigger | Moans; Turn the Tables | Retain if it resolves a direct counter-action, not reflection or event conversion. |
| `SKILL_COMMAND` | Generate or spend the Command resource according to a single declared role | Flirt; BreakResistance; AbsoluteControl | Retain only after deciding whether this is the resource generator or a resource-spend action; avoid a skill that arbitrarily does both. |
| `SKILL_RECOVER_COMPOSURE` | Reduce Strain and/or clear a defined effect | Service; S Symbol; Divine Health | Retain if it offers a clear combat recovery role distinct from ally support and Overflow recovery. |
| `SKILL_EXPLOIT_WEAKNESS` | Consume a tagged weakness for increased payoff and/or Pressure | Sensory Enhancement; Sadism; Aphrodisiac | Retain as a generic exploit primitive only if the specific target-state/tag contract is reusable and not already covered. |
| `SKILL_BREAK_RESISTANCE` | Lower resistance/prepare Break | Hypnotize; Bind; A-penetration; Enslave | Retain as setup rather than a generic disable or outcome. |
| `SKILL_TURN_THE_TABLES` | Convert a defined recent event into a response | Counter; Moans; Get Spanked | Retain if event-history conversion is the distinct mechanic and has a bounded event window. |
| `SKILL_FOCUS_SURGE` | Self-focused temporary boost or resource conversion | Naughty Thoughts; Mstrbate | Retain only after choosing one precise output and duration. |
| `SKILL_DIRECT_ALLY` | Change an ally's next action/target or grant a budgeted action | Encourage; Command | Retain if action selection/commanding differs from a simple next-action buff; never bypass action economy. |
| `SKILL_ABSOLUTE_CONTROL` | Spend substantial control resources to request a permitted encounter outcome | Enslave; Swallow/Bkakke | Retain only if resource conversion and eligibility differ from ordinary finisher thresholds. |
| `SKILL_UNBROKEN` | Prevent one defined defeat/disable event under bounded conditions | Endure; Divine Health | Retain as a rare emergency exception with strict trigger/cooldown/cap; not general immunity. |
| `SKILL_OVERFLOW_ENGINE` | Deliberate conversion of Overflow into one configured output | S Symbol; Ecstasy Aftershocks | Retain if player choice/configuration differs from passive recovery and automatic reaction. |
| `SKILL_MUTUAL_RUIN` | High-risk exchange with explicit consequences on both sides | Summon Tentacles; Bkakke | Retain only if both-side risk is essential to the decision and encounter rules can resolve it deterministically. |

## 6. Proposed cluster decisions

These are recommendations for the next review, not final deletion orders.

### A. Reactive defense and intent
- Keep the concepts separate only if each has a different timing/choice: **Provoke** changes intent; **Get Spanked** invites exposure for Resolve; **Instill protectiveness** redirects protection toward an ally; **Endure** mitigates; **Counter** responds after a trigger; **Moans** reflects; **Turn the Tables** converts recent event history; **Unbroken** prevents one defined defeat condition.
- **Merge candidate:** Moans into Counter if testing shows reflection does not create a distinct build or decision. Do not merge merely because both react to incoming damage.

### B. Control and outcome
- Keep **Command**, **BreakResistance**, and **AbsoluteControl** as separate stages only if their roles are resource/intent management → resistance/Break setup → permitted encounter outcome.
- **Hypnotize**, **Bind**, **A-penetration**, and **Enslave** need distinct roles: intent change, restraint, capability-gated control/setup, and outcome request. Otherwise merge redundant control effects.
- A finisher can request an outcome but cannot independently end the encounter.

### C. Strain, damage-over-time, and vulnerability
- **Bend** is the baseline single-target Strain-over-time candidate; **AS** survives only if its Chain is a genuinely different repeat-use decision.
- **Aphrodisiac** should not carry unrelated damage-over-time and intent changes unless both are one defined state.
- **Pink mist** is the area-over-time candidate; **Flaunt/G-bang** should not remain as duplicate area Strain actions without distinct targeting, risk, cost, or rider.
- **Sensory Enhancement** and **Exploit Weakness** must differ: a persistent vulnerability modifier versus a tagged exploit/Pressure payoff, or one should be consolidated.

### D. Overflow
- **S Symbol** = recovery on Overflow; **Ecstasy Aftershocks** = automatic reactive area output; **Overflow Engine** = deliberate/configured conversion. Each needs a different output and a recursion/cap rule.
- Every effect must declare whether it triggers on entering Overflow, while in Overflow, or once per Overflow event.

### E. Capability and passives
- **P-growth** is a capability/transformation; dependent skills reference its stable tag rather than duplicate narrative conditions.
- **Sadism** and **Masochism** are build passives tied to Pressure/Fortitude loops, not global damage modifiers.
- **Extra tips** is likely economy/reward progression content; **Divine Health** is bounded protection/cleanse, not universal immunity.
- Equipment-linked passives such as **Piercing Stares** must use data-driven predicates.

## 7. Cross-cutting validation checklist before CSV migration

- [ ] Replace H-gauge references with Strain only after effect-by-effect semantic validation.
- [ ] Replace Health Damage with Vitality damage only after checking timing, mitigation, scaling, and outcome interactions.
- [ ] Normalize all Overflow triggers to the shared event contract; prevent accidental recursive triggers.
- [ ] Resolve every legacy row's actor, target set, and target-side inclusion; do not infer Self/Ally/Enemy from prose.
- [ ] Every active skill has explicit action cost, resource costs/generation, requirements, target query, effects, duration/tick rules, and cooldown/chain/threshold/overflow references as applicable.
- [ ] Every passive declares event hooks, scope, stack policy, and whether it is equipment-/capability-/state-gated.
- [ ] Every Capability/Transformation defines granted tag, persistence, removal, target scope, and dependent skill references.
- [ ] All finishers use encounter outcomes; all defeat-prevention effects specify exclusions and caps.
- [ ] Review AoE target counts, friendly fire, and scaling bounds.
- [ ] Compare all conditional/merge candidates in a playable vertical slice before removing thematic identities.
- [ ] Remove the nine unnamed placeholder rows during the actual CSV migration; do not count them as skills.
- [ ] Add stable IDs and structured references only after candidate dispositions are approved.

## 8. Suggested next review sequence

1. Confirm the cluster-level distinctions in section 6 (especially reactive defense, control, and Overflow).
2. Review conditional/merge candidates for single-target and area attacks.
3. Review passive/capability/reward skills.
4. Resolve actor/target and effect semantics.
5. Set tier, Skill Point cost, prerequisites, milestone gates, and numerical tuning.
6. Perform the CSV migration, schema validation, and targeted playtest.

**Current verdict:** skill architecture and meter/capability migration policy are closed; the concrete catalog remains unlocked. This pass narrows the proposed mechanics but does not yet authorize a bulk CSV rewrite.


## Historical reconciliation pass 3 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation, Pass 3: High-Impact Clusters

> Status: **THEMATIC/FUNCTIONAL REVIEW — PROPOSED, NOT YET FINAL CONTENT LOCK**. This pass interprets the legacy skills by their apparent fantasy, action represented, and intended player fantasy, then maps that intent onto the canonical combat system. It does not authorize deleting or rewriting CSV rows.

## 1. Review principle: preserve fantasy, not accidental implementation

## 1A. Approved design decision — per-skill attribute scaling by body part

**Decision confirmed by the project owner:** the body part used by a skill can influence its effectiveness through the attributes associated with that specific skill. Skills that look mechanically similar may therefore remain distinct because they scale from different attributes, body-part associations, requirements, equipment/passive synergies, or progression choices.

- Keep separately unlockable skill records when their action identity and scaling profile support meaningful build choices.
- Reuse shared mechanical templates and effect primitives; shared implementation does not mean merging skill identities.
- Define the relevant scaling attributes and their weights/curves per skill (or via a referenced scaling profile). Do **not** assume a universal one-body-part-to-one-stat mapping.
- Treat body-part/Aspect requirements and attribute scaling as separate data: an Aspect tag alone does not specify the formula.
- If two skills differ only in label/animation and have the same requirements, scaling, effects, costs, and tactical use, they may still be cosmetic variants; this needs to be explicit rather than assumed.
- Numerical coefficients and the exact attribute-to-body-part map remain open content/balance work and are not approved by this decision.


The legacy catalog mixes three layers:
1. **Action fantasy:** what the player imagines Alya doing (tempt, distract, expose herself to danger, protect an ally, endure, retaliate, overwhelm, transform, finish).
2. **Tactical decision:** why the player chooses the skill now (manipulate intent, build a resource, mitigate, apply a state, exploit a threshold, request an outcome).
3. **Legacy effect wording:** old H-gauge/health terminology, mixed target fields, and bundled effects.

The action fantasy is worth preserving when it gives the game personality. It does not, by itself, justify a separate mechanical skill. Two differently themed actions should remain separate if their target, timing, resource loop, risk, Aspect, setup, or outcome creates a different decision. Conversely, two actions that only deal the same Strain damage to the same target with no distinct hook can share a mechanical definition and vary presentation.

All H-gauge references are mapped to Strain and Health Damage to Vitality damage, but each effect must be reconstructed semantically. The thematic labels below are descriptive candidates, not approved final names.

## 2. Cluster A — Intent manipulation, control, and ally protection

This is a high-impact cluster because it determines whether the player is controlling enemy choices, preparing control, or merely inflicting Strain. These should not collapse into a generic “control” action.

| Skill / candidate | Perceived fantasy and action | Recommended tactical identity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flirt | Entice an enemy through charm | Low-cost social/magical action that generates Command, or a modest Strain attack; choose one primary loop | **Conditional retain**: prefer Command generation if Hypnotize owns direct control |
| Hypnotize | Enchant the enemy's mind | Intent manipulation or a defined control state with resistance and duration | **Rework/retain**: no arbitrary bundle of stun + DoT + intent |
| Otherworldly Charm | Make an enemy prefer one kind of behavior/attack | Persistent or passive enemy-intent bias | **Retain** if its scope/duration differs from active Hypnotize |
| Bully | Intimidate and undermine an enemy | Lower offense and/or defense through a named debuff | **Retain**: physical/social intimidation is distinct from mind magic; choose explicit stat effects |
| Bind | Magically restrain the enemy | Apply a restraint state that limits a defined action or movement | **Rework**: state-based control, not generic H-damage |
| A-penetration | Forceful, capability-gated attack that stuns | Focused attack with a control rider or Break setup | **Conditional retain**: if its only result is stun, compare directly with Bind/Hypnotize and merge redundant mechanics |
| Break Resistance | Break down resistance | Setup: lower resistance or prepare Break; should not itself equal a full disable | **Retain as separate proposed addition** if Break is a real intermediate state |
| Command | Command-resource action | Explicitly decide whether this is a generator or a spender; don't let the same skill ambiguously do both | **Needs role selection**; not a second generic control status |
| Absolute Control | High-investment culmination of control | Spend resources and request an encounter-permitted outcome (Disable/Yield/Capture, as authored) | **Conditional retain**: distinct only as a costly outcome conversion, never a global victory command |
| Enslave | Force submission | Thematic finisher that requests a permitted encounter outcome under strict eligibility/resistance rules | **Rework**; compare against Absolute Control. Keep both only if one is Strain-threshold based and the other is Command/Break investment based |
| Worship C | Devotional/ritual action that influences enemy behavior | Area intent bias or vulnerability state | **Choose one primary function**; likely merge with Otherworldly Charm/Aphrodisiac if identical after mechanics are specified |

### Recommended separation
- **Charm/temptation**: Flirt = build Command; Otherworldly Charm = passive/ongoing intent bias.
- **Mind control**: Hypnotize = alter intent or apply a defined temporary mental state.
- **Physical intimidation**: Bully = named stat debuff.
- **Restraint**: Bind = restrict a declared action set.
- **Break setup**: Break Resistance = reduce resistance/prepare Break.
- **Outcome conversion**: Enslave or Absolute Control = request a permitted outcome, not a generic stun.

Do not keep all six as separate entries if their final effect data cannot support these differences.

## 3. Cluster B — Reactive defense, exposure, retaliation, and protection

The intended fantasy here is not just “defend.” The player may invite danger, redirect danger, endure it, or turn it back on the enemy. Those are different tactical identities.

| Skill / candidate | Perceived fantasy and action | Recommended tactical identity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Get Spanked | Alya deliberately invites an enemy attack/exposure | Intentional-risk action; if the enemy resolves the declared trigger, gain Resolve or another bounded reactive benefit | **Retain/rework** as the risk-for-resource action |
| Provoke | Draw the enemy's attention | Change enemy intent/target preference toward a declared actor | **Retain**; it changes who is threatened, not how the threat is mitigated |
| Instill Protectiveness | Encourage an ally to protect someone / redirect aggression | Protection or target-priority effect; the legacy wording says it increases the chance allies are targeted, so resolve the intended beneficiary explicitly | **Rework**; do not implement the ambiguous wording literally |
| Dancing Moves | Dodge gracefully | Active evasion stance with a defined window and action cost | **Retain** as proactive avoidance |
| Endure | Take a hit and remain standing | Active mitigation plus Fortitude generation or a bounded survival benefit | **Retain** as defensive response, not passive reduction |
| Masochism | Become more effective while receiving/withstanding pain | BuildPassive that rewards Fortitude/exposure or grants a conditional defensive benefit | **Rework**; must have trigger/cap and must not duplicate Endure |
| Counter | Respond to an enemy's declared action | Reaction/counter-action after a qualifying trigger, with explicit timing and action budget | **Retain** if a reaction can be resolved deterministically |
| Moans | Turn incoming Strain into reflected Strain | Reflection passive: return a bounded portion of the qualifying incoming effect | **Conditional retain**; merge into Counter only if reflection offers no distinct build choice |
| Turn the Tables | Convert what just happened into a reversal | Consume a specific recent-event record to respond, recover, or retaliate | **Retain** if event-history conversion is materially different from a direct counter |
| Unbroken | Refuse a defeat or disabling event | One narrowly defined emergency prevention effect with cooldown/once-per-encounter constraints | **Conditional retain**; must not create general immunity |
| Divine Health | Exceptional resilience and recovery | Conditional resistance or a restricted cleanse, not blanket immunity to all debuffs | **Rework** as support/passive protection |
| S Symbol | Recover when Strain overflows | Recovery triggered by an Overflow event | **Retain** if it restores a distinct channel (choose Vitality, Strain reduction, or a specific cleanse) |
| Ecstasy Aftershocks | Overflow spills into an area reaction | Automatic, bounded area effect on a single Overflow event | **Retain** as reactive offensive build hook |
| Overflow Engine | Intentionally convert Overflow into a chosen result | Active/configurable conversion of an Overflow event into one declared output | **Retain as proposed addition** only if player choice differs from automatic reaction/recovery |

### Recommended reactive loop
1. **Provoke** determines who the enemy intends to attack.
2. **Dancing Moves** avoids an attack before it lands.
3. **Endure** reduces the consequences of an incoming hit and may generate Fortitude.
4. **Get Spanked** is a deliberate exposure choice that generates Resolve when its condition actually resolves.
5. **Counter** reacts to a defined event; **Moans** reflects; **Turn the Tables** uses event history. These remain separate only if their timing/output differs.
6. **Unbroken** is an emergency exception, not another mitigation layer.

Avoid stacking multiple passive “take less damage” effects without caps or diminishing returns. Reactive skills need a precise trigger window and deterministic ordering.

## 4. Cluster C — Strain application, damage-over-time, vulnerability, and finishers

This cluster is the greatest risk for duplicate actions because many legacy skills describe a thematic action but only specify “H-Damage.” Preserve distinct animations and thematic actions, but require a distinct tactical hook for a separate mechanical record.

| Skill / candidate | Perceived fantasy and action | Recommended tactical identity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sensory Enhancement | Heighten sensitivity/vulnerability | Apply a tagged vulnerability effect to a named channel (Strain or Vitality, or both only if explicitly justified) | **Rework**; compare with Exploit Weakness |
| Sensory Deprivation | Overwhelm or suppress senses | Debuff a named subset of accuracy/evasion/defense | **Retain**; multi-stat effect needs cost/duration justification |
| Bend | Prolonged single-target pressure | Baseline single-target Strain-over-time | **Retain** as the simplest DoT candidate |
| AS | Escalate pressure when repeated | Chain/sequence mechanic that rewards repeated use and defines reset conditions | **Conditional retain**; not merely a stronger Bend |
| Aphrodisiac | Sustain Strain and influence behavior | Applied state with explicit tick effect and/or intent bias | **Rework**; preserve both only if the combined state is coherent |
| Pink Mist | Spread prolonged pressure to many enemies | Area Strain-over-time plus one named defense debuff | **Retain** as the area DoT candidate |
| Flaunt | Draw attention and overwhelm the group | Area Strain action with an explicit target filter or rider | **Conditional retain** vs G-bang; no generic duplicate AoE |
| G-bang | Group-focused overwhelming action | Area Strain attack | **Merge candidate** with Flaunt if no different risk/targeting/rider exists |
| Summon C-S | Magical group-wide pressure that scales with target count | Summoned area action with declared scaling, target set, and friendly-fire policy | **Retain/rework** if the summon/scaling decision is unique |
| Summon Tentacles | Uncontrolled otherworldly force threatens everyone | High-risk area action affecting explicitly listed sides; retaliation/friendly fire is core to the fantasy | **Retain/rework** if the risk is real, bounded, and deterministic |
| Spank / Bite | Focused physical attacks using different Aspects | Basic focused attack candidates | **Conditional**: keep separately only if Aspect, trigger, cost, or state interaction matters |
| Bl-job / Lay / Grope / H-job / Paizuri / Deepth | Different intimate actions, but legacy data mostly gives the same Strain damage role | Shared baseline action family with distinct presentation/Aspect variants unless one has a unique tactical effect | **Consolidate mechanics first**; do not assume six separate skill records are justified by animation alone |
| Facesit | Smother the target with a clear self-positioning risk | Focused action with a self-exposure/positioning consequence only if mechanically real | **Conditional retain**; correct actor/target schema |
| Prolonged Pl- | Sustain pressure at a full Strain state and punish further actions | Threshold/Overflow interaction with a clear trigger and ordered Vitality-damage consequence | **Rework**; the legacy text both prevents Overflow and refers to a filled gauge—resolve the intended state machine |
| Stomp | Punish a highly strained target with Vitality damage | Threshold-based conversion from Strain state to Vitality damage | **Retain** if Strain level changes the action's role, not just its output number |
| Swallow | Finish a weakened target | Single-target threshold finisher that requests a permitted encounter outcome | **Retain/rework**; threshold must be explicit and encounter-defined |
| Bkakke | Attempt a group finisher and suffer a consequence if anyone survives | All-target threshold finisher with a clear survivor-risk clause | **Rework/conditional**; resolve each target deterministically and never directly force combat victory |
| Strip | Area pressure paired with evasive flair | AoE Strain plus evasion only if the combination creates a coherent risk/reward turn | **Conditional retain** vs Flaunt/G-bang; split or remove unrelated rider if not |
| F-fk | High-risk capability-gated action that becomes stronger against a weakened target | Capability-gated execute/Strain scaling with a bounded Vitality threshold | **Conditional retain**; declare whose Vitality is checked and cap the scaling |
| Penetrate | Capability-gated focused action | Focused Strain attack with a distinct capability fantasy | **Conditional** vs A-penetration; keep both only if the latter's control rider materially changes tactical role |
| Cuteness | Supportive appeal that boosts allies | Passive ally attack buff with defined scope and stacking | **Retain/rework**; compare with Encourage |
| Encourage | Directly inspire an ally | Active next-action buff | **Retain**; action vs passive distinction is meaningful if durations/economy differ |
| Service | Intimate support directed at an ally | Restore one explicitly named channel or remove a whitelisted effect | **Rework**; don't leave generic “heals ally” |
| C-drinker | Recover after an enemy is defeated using a certain action family | Event-triggered recovery passive | **Retain/rework** if the trigger rewards a build; exact recovery channel required |

### Proposed baseline attack policy
Create a small number of shared mechanical templates (e.g. Focused Strain Attack, Focused Vitality Attack, Single-Target DoT, Area Strain, Area DoT, Threshold Conversion, Finisher Request). Presentation/Aspect variants can reuse them. Promote a variant to a separate skill only if it changes a tactical decision, unlock, resource loop, risk, or effect.

This is not a command to delete the legacy action names; it is a recommendation to separate mechanical identity from presentation identity.

## 5. Cluster D — Capability, passives, and economy

| Skill | Perceived fantasy and action | Recommended tactical identity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-growth | Transform/gain a new capability that enables other actions | Capability/Transformation granting CAPABILITY_P_GROWTH | **Retain/reclassify**; target scope, persistence, acquisition, and removal remain unresolved |
| Autof | Use the gained capability for self-recovery and cleansing | Capability-gated recovery action; choose Vitality restoration, Strain reduction, and/or a limited cleanse explicitly | **Retain/rework** |
| Extra Tips | Earn better rewards after victory | Economy/reward passive triggered by a declared victory/reward event | **Move out of combat catalog** unless passives intentionally live there as a unified catalog |
| Piercing Stares | Equipment-specific pressure from piercings | Equipment-predicate passive applying a declared effect | **Retain/rework**; use equipment tags rather than hard-coded item IDs |
| Sadism | Gain offensive benefit from a particular playstyle/state | BuildPassive tied to Pressure/exploited states | **Retain/rework**; cap modifier and declare triggers |
| Naughty Thoughts | Mental focus increases accuracy/effectiveness of certain actions | BuildPassive restricted to explicit action tags and bounded modifiers | **Retain/rework** |
| Mstrbate | Self-directed recovery combined with influencing enemy behavior | Split candidate: self-recovery and enemy-intent manipulation are separate decisions unless a single risk/reward loop links them | **Rework**; don't keep the bundled effect merely because the theme is coherent |
| Divine Health | Protect allies from harmful effects while helping them recover | Conditional resistance or targeted cleanse support | **Rework**; avoid blanket debuff immunity |

## 6. High-impact disposition summary

### Strong candidates to retain as distinct mechanics
- Dancing Moves (proactive evasion)
- Hypnotize (intent/state control)
- Bully (named stat debuff)
- Bind (restraint state)
- Break Resistance (setup)
- Provoke (intent redirection)
- Get Spanked (deliberate exposure/Resolve)
- Endure (mitigation/Fortitude)
- Counter (triggered response)
- Turn the Tables (event-history conversion)
- Bend (baseline DoT)
- Pink Mist (area DoT)
- Summon Tentacles (explicit high-risk area action)
- Stomp (Strain-threshold Vitality conversion)
- Swallow (single-target outcome request)
- P-growth (capability)

“Strong candidate” does not mean final numbers or requirements are approved.

### Conditional/merge-review candidates
- Flirt vs Hypnotize
- Instill Protectiveness vs Provoke
- Moans vs Counter
- AS vs Bend
- Sensory Enhancement vs Exploit Weakness
- Aphrodisiac vs Otherworldly Charm/Worship C
- Flaunt vs G-bang vs Strip
- Spank/Bite and the mostly flavor-based single-target actions
- Penetrate vs A-penetration
- Enslave vs Absolute Control
- S Symbol vs Ecstasy Aftershocks vs Overflow Engine (retain only as distinct recovery/reactive/active conversion roles)
- Cuteness vs Encourage
- Mstrbate's recovery vs intent effect

### Rules to apply before migration
- No skill should directly force global combat victory.
- No finisher may bypass the encounter's outcome contract.
- Every reaction needs a deterministic trigger, trigger window, and action/resource budget.
- Every DoT needs tick timing, duration, refresh/stack rules, and expiry.
- Every debuff needs named affected stats/states and stacking policy.
- Every area skill needs a legal target set, target-count scaling, and friendly-fire rule.
- Every capability-gated skill needs a declarative capability requirement.
- Every passive needs a defined event hook or predicate and bounded output.
- Separate Strain and Vitality effects; “heal” must specify its channel.
- Keep thematic labels where useful, but don't equate every presentation animation with a unique mechanical skill.

## 7. Recommended review decisions for the next user pass

The highest-value decisions to close next are:
1. **Control/outcome:** should Enslave and Absolute Control be distinct via different resource loops (Strain threshold vs Command/Break investment), or should one be the sole top-tier control finisher?
2. **Basic intimate action family:** should baseline actions that differ only by animation/Aspect be separate unlockable skills, or be presentation variants of shared mechanics?
3. **AoE identity:** should Flaunt/G-bang/Strip become one base AoE family with variants, while Summon C-S and Summon Tentacles remain distinct due to scaling/risk?
4. **Overflow:** keep three roles (recovery, reactive AoE, active conversion) or reduce to two if active conversion cannot offer a meaningful choice?
5. **P-growth target scope:** self-only transformation, or a capability that can be granted to allies? The old CSV says Ally but the described fantasy is a transformation; this is not safe to infer.

No answers are assumed by this document. The concrete catalog remains unlocked until these questions are resolved.


## Historical reconciliation pass 4 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation Pass 4
## Focused single-target actions: scaling profiles, effect identity, and redundancy

> **Status:** Decision-updated proposal. The project owner's six Pass 4 decisions are recorded below. This pass does not change data/skills/skills.csv, approve numerical coefficients, or lock the final catalog.

## 1. Decisions already treated as constraints

### 1A. Project-owner decisions — Pass 4

1. **Basic attacks: B.** Keep separate skill records when body-part affinity/scaling supports meaningful build choices; reuse shared effect templates.
2. **Spank/Bite: B.** Each has one primary damage channel; a second channel is permitted only as an explicit conditional rider with ordered effects.
3. **Flirt: C.** Flirt generates Command and deals Strain damage. This combined role needs explicit tuning/cost and should not outperform dedicated damage or resource actions without trade-offs.
4. **Lay/Facesit risk: B.** Self-risk is applied only when it is an intentional, explicit trade-off with a defined payoff.
5. **AS/Bend: A.** AS remains a Chain/sequence skill with explicit reset conditions; if no meaningful Chain behavior can be defined, consolidate it with Bend.
6. **H-job: B.** Keep the Mind Aspect and revise the intended effect to a genuine mental/sensory interaction; do not silently change it to a physical body part.

These choices resolve the design direction, but exact numerical balance and detailed effect conditions remain open.

- The eight primary attributes remain BODY, AGI, SOUL, CHARM, DOM, SUB, SAD, and MAS.
- The body-part affinity used by a skill is a separate scaling input. It can change the effectiveness of otherwise similar actions.
- There is no universal body-part-to-attribute mapping. Each skill or reusable scaling profile declares its own contributors.
- Strain damage and Vitality damage are separate effect channels. Legacy labels must be interpreted semantically, not replaced blindly.
- A shared effect template does not require merging skills if their scaling, body-part affinity, prerequisites, costs, risk, trigger, target state, or synergies make different build choices.
- Names and animations alone do not justify separate mechanical records.
- No coefficients, caps, durations, costs, or unlock prerequisites are approved by this pass.

## 2. Proposed reusable scaling-profile shape

Each profile should declare:
1. **Primary attribute(s):** main contributor(s) to effectiveness.
2. **Secondary attribute(s):** smaller/conditional contributor(s), if any.
3. **Body-part affinity:** one part or an explicitly defined composite.
4. **Effect channel:** Strain, Vitality, mixed, recovery, control, or support.
5. **Context hook:** e.g. target state, vulnerability, consecutive use, P-growth, or threshold.
6. **Guardrails:** cap, resistance check, action-economy rule, and/or explicit target validation.

The words “primary” and “secondary” indicate a proposed profile structure, not numerical weights. Do not assume all listed attributes contribute to every effect in the same way. A future formula should allow different attribute contributions for hit chance, Strain impact, Vitality impact, duration, and control strength where the design needs that distinction.

## 3. Focused-action review matrix

### A. Basic Strain-focused actions

| Skill | Proposed profile | Core effect | Why it may remain distinct | Main unresolved point |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bl-job | CHARM + SUB; Mouth | Focused Strain attack | Mouth affinity and a CHARM/SUB profile may support a different build from other actions | Does it have any unique cost, requirement, state interaction, or only a unique profile/animation? |
| Lay | SUB + MAS; Crotch | Focused Strain attack; self-risk only if explicitly authored as a trade-off | SUB/MAS and Crotch affinity can support a distinct build choice | If self-risk is included, define its exact trigger, consequence and payoff; otherwise resolve only against the target. |
| Grope | CHARM + AGI; Ass + Breasts composite | Focused Strain attack | A defined multi-part composite can support a distinct affinity build | Should the composite use a fixed declared formula, a chosen part, or be reduced to one part? |
| H-job | AGI + CHARM; Mind Aspect | Focused Strain action that targets or exploits a defined mental/sensory state | Accuracy/precision plus Mind interaction distinguishes it from ordinary focused attacks | Rewrite the legacy description/effect to define the mental/sensory interaction; do not treat Mind as a physical body part. |
| Paizuri | CHARM + SUB; Breasts | Focused Strain attack | Breast affinity gives a distinct scaling route from Mouth/Crotch/Ass actions | Is affinity alone enough to justify its own unlock, given the action uses the shared basic-attack template? |
| Deepth | SUB + MAS; Mouth | Focused Strain attack | Could reward high SUB/MAS and Mouth affinity | Needs a concrete tactical distinction from Bl-job; otherwise one can be a presentation variant. |
| Flirt | CHARM + DOM; Mouth | Generate Command **and** deal Strain damage | Hybrid pressure/resource action; supports the Command → Break → Control loop while still contributing direct pressure | Explicitly budget its action cost, Command yield and Strain output so it does not dominate dedicated generators or attacks. |

**Recommendation:** Use one shared FocusedStrainAttack effect template, with a per-skill scaling profile. Retain separate unlockable actions when the body-part affinity, attribute profile, prerequisite, cost, risk, or tactical hook creates a meaningful choice. Do not create a bespoke effect handler per animation.

### B. Focused physical / mixed-channel actions

| Skill | Proposed profile | Core effect | Why it may remain distinct | Main unresolved point |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Spank | BODY + SAD; Ass | One primary channel; any second channel is an explicit conditional rider | Ass affinity and BODY/SAD scaling support a physical specialization | Choose the primary channel during final effect definition; specify the rider trigger and ordering if a second channel remains. |
| Bite | BODY + SAD; Mouth | One primary channel; any second channel is an explicit conditional rider | Mouth affinity can create a different scaling path from Spank | Choose the primary channel during final effect definition; specify the rider trigger and ordering if a second channel remains. |
| Facesit | BODY + SUB; Crotch | Focused action with one primary channel; self-risk only if explicitly authored as a trade-off | Can trade actor exposure for pressure on a vulnerable target | The legacy Enemy + Self target field is not itself a self-effect; declare any self-cost separately, or remove Self from the target query. |
| Penetrate | BODY + DOM; Crotch; requires P-growth | Focused Strain attack | Capability gate and Crotch affinity create a progression specialization | Is it a reliable Strain attack, or should it have a specific interaction with an existing state? |
| A-penetration | BODY + DOM; Crotch; requires P-growth | Focused attack plus a defined control/Break rider | Different from Penetrate if the rider changes turn planning or enables a setup chain | Recommendation: use a named control/Break effect with resistance and duration; avoid an undefined “stun”. |
| F-fk | BODY + DOM; Crotch; requires P-growth | Strain attack that scales against a target below a Vitality threshold | Rewards timing and target selection rather than being a flat upgrade | Define threshold, whose Vitality is checked, scaling cap, and whether it also deals Vitality damage. |
| Get Spanked | MAS + SUB; Ass | Deliberate exposure; gain Resolve after a qualifying enemy event resolves | Not a direct attack: converts chosen risk into future reactive options | Confirm the reward trigger is an actual resolved incoming event, not merely selecting the action. |
| Mstrbate | MAS + CHARM; Crotch | Self-recovery **or** enemy-intent influence | Could create self-sustain or a control loop | Recommendation: choose one main effect per skill. If both are retained, specify a real cost/risk trade-off and separate ordered effects. |

**Recommendation:** Separate the hit/effect calculation from channel conversion. A skill may use a shared hit/scaling profile and then apply Strain, Vitality, or both as explicit ordered effects. Do not infer that “Physical” means Vitality damage or “Magical” means Strain damage.

### C. Single-target sustained damage and control

| Skill | Proposed profile | Core effect | Why it may remain distinct | Main unresolved point |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bend | SUB + MAS; Ass | Baseline single-target Strain-over-time | Establishes the basic DoT loop | Define tick timing, duration, refresh, stacking, and expiry in the companion effect definition. |
| AS | SUB + MAS; Ass | Chain/sequence effect tied to consecutive use, with explicit reset conditions | Sequence commitment creates a distinct tactical decision from baseline Bend | Define what counts as consecutive use, which events reset the chain, how the effect changes, and how duration/stacking interact. If no meaningful rule survives, merge into Bend. |
| Sensory Enhancement | SOUL + CHARM; Mind | Apply a tagged vulnerability to a named channel | Sets up Exploit/Pressure and follow-up skills rather than dealing direct damage | Name the vulnerability target: Strain, Vitality, a specific action tag, or a registered damage family. |
| Hypnotize | SOUL + DOM; Mind | Change intent or apply a defined control state | Alters enemy options; not just damage-over-time | Recommendation: remove the ambiguous “stun + DoT” bundle. Choose intent change or a registered control state as primary. |
| Bind | SOUL + DOM; Mind | Restraint/control state limiting declared actions | Can set up Break Resistance, Hypnotize, or outcome requests | Define exactly which action categories are restricted and how the target resists/escapes. The current scaling audit accidentally omitted Bind; it belongs in the control review. |
| Bully | CHARM + SAD; Mouth | Intimidation debuff to named offensive stats | Reduces capability rather than changing intent or applying control | Choose attack effectiveness and/or accuracy; avoid vague “attack and defense” without registered stat IDs. |

**Recommendation:** Treat damage-over-time, vulnerability, intent change, and action restriction as separate effect families. They can share scaling inputs where appropriate, but their state semantics and counterplay should not be conflated.

## 4. Candidate shared effect templates

1. FocusedStrainAttack: target query + hit/accuracy + Strain impact + optional declared rider.
2. FocusedVitalityAttack: target query + hit/accuracy + Vitality impact + mitigation.
3. FocusedMixedChannelAttack: ordered Strain and Vitality effects; each channel declares its own scaling/mitigation rule.
4. ApplyStrainDoT: apply registered effect with explicit tick/duration/refresh/stack rules.
5. ApplyVulnerability: apply a named vulnerability tag/channel with stacking/cap rules.
6. ChangeIntent: deterministic intent-selection modification with eligibility and duration.
7. ApplyControlState: registered state that restricts named action categories, with resistance and expiry.
8. GenerateResourceOnResolvedEvent: resource generation tied to a named event, never to ambiguous action selection alone.

These are proposed templates, not necessarily new runtime effect types. Reuse the existing canonical primitives where possible.

## 5. Cross-skill synergies

- **Command → Break → Control:** Flirt/Command generates Command → Break Resistance lowers a named resistance or adds Break progress → Hypnotize changes intent or Bind restricts declared actions → Enslave/Absolute Control requests an encounter-permitted outcome.
- **Affinity build → specialized attack:** body-part affinity and a declared attribute profile make Bl-job, Paizuri, Deepth, Spank, and Bite valid alternative investments without requiring unique effect code.
- **Exposure → Resolve → reaction:** Get Spanked deliberately accepts a qualifying event → generates Resolve → Counter or Turn the Tables spends/reacts to that opportunity.
- **DoT → vulnerability → exploitation:** Bend/AS applies persistent Strain pressure → Sensory Enhancement exposes a named channel → Exploit Weakness or a threshold action converts the opening.
- **Capability → focused follow-up:** P-growth grants a stable capability → Penetrate becomes available; A-penetration adds a defined control/Break rider; F-fk adds a capped low-Vitality target condition.
- **Control → outcome request:** Bind/Hypnotize create a defined control state → Command/Break investment may enable Enslave or Absolute Control, subject to encounter eligibility and resistance.

## 6. Decisions requested before numerical balancing

### Decision 1 — Similar basic attacks — DECIDED: B
Keep separate skill records where per-skill body-part affinity/scaling and progression are intended build choices, but share the same effect template.

- A. Merge mechanically identical basic attacks into one skill with presentation variants; affinity does not justify separate unlocks.
- **B. Shared effect template, separate unlockable skills when scaling/affinity creates a meaningful build choice.**
- C. Separate only if each has an additional unique status, resource hook, risk, or tactical rule; scaling alone is not enough.

### Decision 2 — Spank and Bite — DECIDED: B
Give each one clear primary channel and only add a second channel as an explicit conditional rider.

- A. Both always deal Strain and Vitality damage.
- **B. Each has one primary damage channel; any second channel is conditional and explicitly ordered.**
- C. One is Strain-focused and the other Vitality-focused.

### Decision 3 — Flirt — DECIDED: C
Flirt generates Command and deals Strain damage. Its resource yield and damage output must be balanced together, with explicit costs and limits.

- A. Generate Command, with negligible/no direct damage.
- B. Deal Strain damage; another skill generates Command.
- **C. Generate Command and deal Strain damage, with explicit tuning to prevent it dominating either role.**

### Decision 4 — Lay and Facesit risk — DECIDED: B
Only apply self-risk when it is an explicit mechanical trade-off.

- A. Both are reliable focused attacks without self-risk.
- **B. Self-risk only on the action whose fantasy/identity requires it, with a declared payoff.**
- C. Both are exposure actions that generate Resolve or another resource.

### Decision 5 — AS versus Bend — DECIDED: A
AS stays separate as a Chain/sequence skill with explicit reset conditions.

- **A. Keep AS as a Chain mechanic; otherwise merge it into Bend.**
- B. Merge both into one DoT skill.
- C. Keep both as independent DoTs with different duration/stacking profiles.

### Decision 6 — H-job body-part field — DECIDED: B
Keep Mind because the intended mechanic targets a mental/sensory Aspect, and revise the description/effect accordingly.

- A. Correct the body-part field to a physical part.
- **B. Keep Mind and revise the description/effect to define the mental/sensory interaction.**
- C. Leave it unresolved until visual/animation design.

## 7. Not yet approved

- Attribute coefficients/curves, affinity multipliers, hit formulas, costs, and caps.
- Exact unlock tiers/prerequisites.
- Whether affinity alone warrants separate Skill Point costs.
- Exact primary channels and any conditional second-channel riders for Spank/Bite/Facesit.
- H-job's precise mental/sensory effect and target eligibility (Mind is now retained as the intended Aspect).
- Flirt's numerical balance between Command generation and Strain damage.
- AS's exact Chain progression and reset conditions.
- P-growth target scope, persistence, and revocation.
- Final merges between similar basic-action variants beyond the decisions above.

## 8. Acceptance criteria for the next pass

Before numeric balance:
- Every focused action has an unambiguous actor, target, channel, and effect order.
- Each scaling profile identifies attribute contributors and body-part affinity without invented universal mappings.
- Similar skills are either justified as distinct build choices or marked for consolidation.
- Control states, DoTs, vulnerability, and resource triggers use registered deterministic effect semantics.
- All skill records point to stable scaling/effect/targeting references when the catalog schema migration begins.


## Historical reconciliation pass 5 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation Pass 5
## Area actions, finishers, Overflow, and encounter-outcome boundaries

> **Status:** Updated with the project owner's six decisions. This document does not modify data/skills/skills.csv, approve numeric coefficients, or finalize the skill catalog.

## 1. Constraints carried forward from Pass 4

- Basic attacks may remain separate unlockable skills when body-part affinity/scaling creates meaningful build choices; use shared effect templates.
- Spank and Bite each have one primary damage channel. A second channel requires an explicit conditional rider and ordered effect resolution.
- Flirt generates Command and deals Strain damage. Its cost, resource yield, and damage output must be balanced together.
- Lay/Facesit self-risk exists only when explicitly designed as a trade-off with a declared payoff.
- AS remains a Chain/sequence skill with explicit reset conditions; if its Chain has no meaningful tactical behavior, consolidate with Bend.
- H-job retains Mind as its intended Aspect and must be rewritten as a genuine mental/sensory interaction.
- Strain and Vitality are distinct channels. Finishers request an encounter-permitted outcome; they do not bypass the encounter-outcome contract.
- CSV remains unchanged until ambiguities are resolved.

## 2. Area-action review

| Skill | Proposed identity | Required semantics | Distinctness recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flaunt | Area Strain pressure | Explicit target query, hit/accuracy policy, per-target scaling, resistance, and one declared rider at most | **Owner decision: A — retain as a mechanically distinct action.** Define the unique target rule, scaling profile, cost, or tactical rider that justifies the distinction; do not rely on label/animation alone. |
| G-bang | Area Strain pressure | Explicit target query, per-target scaling, and any unique target filter/risk | **Owner decision: A — retain as a mechanically distinct action.** Give it a concrete difference from Flaunt (target eligibility, scaling, cost/risk, or effect rider) before catalog lock. |
| Strip | Area Strain plus temporary evasion | Resolve the area effect and evasion effect in a fixed order; define evasion duration and eligible incoming attacks | **Owner decision: A — retain as a mechanically distinct action.** The temporary evasion window must be a real tactical distinction with defined duration and eligible incoming attacks. |
| Summon c-s | Area Strain summon that concentrates as fewer enemies remain | Target set; formula/curve for target-count scaling; minimum/maximum output; behavior when targets die or enter/leave the set; friendly-fire policy | Keep distinct for target-count scaling. Do not let damage rise without a floor/cap or a defined number of eligible enemies. |
| Summon Tentacles | HighRisk area action with explicit side exposure | Explicit affected sides and targets; per-target resolution; self/ally consequences; target-count scaling; interrupt/resistance/counterplay; recursion guard if it triggers other effects | Keep distinct from Summon c-s because the risk to allies/self is central. “Aggro everyone” is not a complete target or effect rule. |
| Pink Mist | Area Strain DoT plus named defense debuff | Explicit DoT tick/duration/refresh/stack rules and separately named debuff with its own duration/stacking/cleanse rules | Keep distinct from direct area attacks because persistent pressure and debuff maintenance create different decisions. |
| Worship C | Area intent bias OR named vulnerability/debuff | Select one primary function; explicit eligible targets and duration; avoid bundling an area attack, intent change, and vulnerability without a coherent sequence | Keep if it offers a group-control/ritual role distinct from Otherworldly Charm and Aphrodisiac; otherwise consolidate the redundant effect. |

### Recommended shared templates

- `AreaStrainAttack`: target query + per-target hit/impact + target-count rule + declared optional rider.
- `AreaStrainDoT`: target query + per-target application of a registered DoT + explicit tick/stack/expiry policy.
- `AreaHighRiskAction`: explicit target sets on each side + deterministic per-target resolution + self/ally risk + caps/counterplay.
- `AreaIntentEffect`: declared intent change/bias over eligible targets with duration and resistance.
- `ApplyTemporaryEvasion`: explicit attack eligibility, duration, and timing.

These are logical templates, not automatic requests to add custom runtime handlers. Reuse canonical primitives where possible.

## 3. Area scaling and targeting rules to close

1. **Target selection:** distinguish “all enemies,” “all combatants,” “all valid targets,” and “all other actors.” Never infer target sides from a display label.
2. **Per-target calculation:** each target resolves its own accuracy, resistance, damage channel, vulnerability, and outcome check unless the effect explicitly specifies a shared roll.
3. **Target-count scaling:** declare whether output scales per target, inversely with target count, or concentrates a fixed budget across remaining targets. Define min/max and rounding.
4. **Friendly fire:** if allies/self are eligible, state whether they receive the full effect, a reduced effect, a different channel, or a separate consequence. No implicit friendly fire.
5. **Deaths/disable during resolution:** define whether the target list is snapshotted at action start or recomputed after each target; recommendation: snapshot eligible targets at action resolution start, then skip actors no longer valid when their turn in the list arrives.
6. **Ordering:** target ordering must be deterministic, such as stable combatant ID order after the target query is evaluated.
7. **Riders:** a defensive or debuff rider should apply once to the actor or separately per target, explicitly. Do not accidentally multiply a self-buff by target count.
8. **Overflow triggers:** an area effect that causes multiple targets to cross Strain thresholds must specify whether each target can trigger an Overflow event independently and how chained effects are capped.

## 4. Finisher and outcome review

| Skill | Proposed identity | Required semantics | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Swallow | Single-target finisher request at a Strain/state threshold | Eligibility predicate, resistance/contest, outcome request, fallback if target is ineligible, and resource/cost behavior on failure | Keep as a focused threshold finisher. Request only an encounter-permitted outcome such as Disable, Capture, Yield, or another explicitly authored result. |
| Bkakke | Multi-target finisher request with strict all-target victory condition | Every enemy must be eligible and every outcome request must succeed for the encounter to count as a victory; resolve each target deterministically and define failed/ineligible-target behavior | **Owner decision: A — all-or-nothing encounter victory.** The skill cannot directly force victory; if any target is ineligible or any request fails, the encounter continues under the encounter outcome contract. Whether successful per-target outcomes persist when another target fails remains an explicit Pass 6 decision. |
| Enslave | Shared top-tier control/finisher family | Use the same canonical eligibility, resistance, duration, permitted-outcome, and failure contract as Absolute Control | **Owner decision: A — merge with Absolute Control** into one top-tier finisher family. Distinct names/presentation may remain as variants only if they do not create duplicate mechanical entries; requirements and scaling should be data-driven. |
| Absolute Control | Shared top-tier control/finisher family | Use the same canonical eligibility, resistance, duration, permitted-outcome, and failure contract as Enslave; retain Command/Break as the shared setup route if confirmed | **Owner decision: A — merge with Enslave** into one top-tier finisher family. Keep one mechanical identity and allow named variants only when requirements/scaling differ meaningfully. |
| Stomp | Convert target Strain threshold into Vitality damage | Threshold, conversion cap, mitigation order, target validity, and whether the threshold is consumed | Keep distinct: it converts progress between damage channels rather than requesting an encounter outcome. |
| F-fk | Capped bonus against low-Vitality target | State whose Vitality is checked, threshold, scaling cap, order relative to mitigation, and interaction with Strain threshold | Keep distinct from a generic attack only if the threshold window changes target priority and timing. |

### Outcome contract for every finisher

A finisher must declare:
- the target predicate and threshold/state requirement;
- resistance or contest procedure;
- the exact requested outcome, chosen from the encounter's permitted outcomes;
- whether the request can partially succeed for area finishers;
- resource/cost handling on success and failure;
- fallback behavior when the target becomes invalid before resolution;
- effects on surviving targets and the user;
- deterministic event ordering.

A finisher may request an outcome but may not independently end the encounter or override an encounter's allowed outcomes.

## 5. Overflow cluster review

| Skill | Proposed role | Trigger/output contract | Keep distinct if… |
|---|---|---|---|
| S Symbol | Passive recovery | One resolved Overflow event triggers one declared recovery output: restore Vitality, reduce Strain, or remove an eligible effect | It chooses a different recovery channel from the other Overflow options or has a distinct trigger/eligibility profile. |
| Ecstasy Aftershocks | Passive area retaliation | One resolved Overflow event produces a bounded area effect against a declared target set; cannot recursively trigger itself | Automatic offensive retaliation is distinct from recovery and requires no player choice after activation. |
| Overflow Engine | Active/configurable conversion | Converts a resolved Overflow event into one selected output; declare selection timing, allowed outputs, cost, cap, and recursion behavior | Player configuration materially changes the build or response. If it merely duplicates one passive output, merge or redesign. |
| Prolonged Pl- | Threshold/Overflow manipulation | Must choose a single coherent transition: prevent Overflow, lock at a threshold, or convert qualifying follow-up actions. No contradictory combination | Keep only if the transition creates a clear control strategy distinct from ordinary Strain damage. |

### Recommended Overflow event contract

1. Determine Strain changes from the resolved action.
2. Evaluate whether each affected actor crossed the defined Overflow boundary.
3. Emit one resolved Overflow event per qualifying actor and threshold crossing according to the canonical meter rules.
4. Trigger eligible listeners in deterministic priority order.
5. Prevent a listener from recursively triggering itself indefinitely; enforce explicit chain depth/output caps.
6. Apply recovery/retaliation/conversion effects and then evaluate encounter outcomes.
7. If multiple listeners compete for the same event, each must specify whether it observes the original event or a modified state; do not consume the same event implicitly.

This sequence is a proposal for content definition and must be reconciled with the canonical turn-resolution contract before implementation.

## 6. Cross-skill synergies

- **Area pressure → persistent control:** Flaunt/G-bang → Pink Mist → vulnerability or intent effect → threshold conversion.
- **Risk/reward summon:** Summon Tentacles exposes declared sides to risk → Fortitude/mitigation builds reduce the cost → area pressure can accelerate Strain thresholds on both sides.
- **Concentrated summon:** Summon c-s becomes stronger against fewer eligible enemies → use target priority to finish a dangerous survivor rather than maximizing raw total damage.
- **Threshold conversion:** Bend/AS/Pink Mist build Strain → Stomp converts a defined threshold into Vitality progress; Swallow requests an allowed outcome.
- **Single vs multi-target finisher:** Swallow focuses setup on one eligible target; Bkakke requires group threshold preparation and carries survivor risk.
- **Overflow build choice:** S Symbol for recovery, Ecstasy Aftershocks for automatic retaliation, Overflow Engine for configured conversion.
- **Control investment:** Command generation → Break setup → Enslave/Absolute Control outcome request, with encounter-specific eligibility and failure handling.

## 7. Decisions requested before numerical balancing

### Decision 1 — Flaunt, G-bang, and Strip — DECIDED: A
Keep all three as mechanically distinct skills. They may reuse the same implementation template, but each must have a documented mechanical differentiator beyond name/animation.

- **A. Keep all three as mechanically unique actions.**
- B. Shared area template; Strip keeps its defensive rider if balanced.
- C. Merge all three into one skill with presentation variants.

### Decision 2 — Summon c-s scaling — DECIDED: A
Preserve the “fewer enemies = stronger summon” identity, with an explicit curve, floor/cap, and a per-target rule.

- **A. Concentrates output against fewer valid enemies; define curve and cap.**
- B. Fixed total output distributed across current targets.
- C. Remove target-count scaling and differentiate it through another tactical hook.

### Decision 3 — Summon Tentacles friendly fire — DECIDED: B
Declare affected sides/targets and resolve any friendly-fire consequences deterministically.

- A. Always affects all combatants equally.
- **B. Declared target sides and deterministic friendly-fire consequences.**
- C. Enemies only; “aggro everyone” becomes visual flavor, not mechanical targeting.

### Decision 4 — Bkakke — DECIDED: A
Encounter victory is permitted only when every enemy is eligible and every per-target outcome request succeeds. If any enemy is ineligible or any request fails, the encounter continues; successful per-target outcomes and consequences on failure still need an explicit atomicity rule in Pass 6.

- **A. Combat victory only if every enemy is eligible and the request succeeds on every target.**
- B. Per-target outcomes; survivor consequence when one or more targets remain.
- C. Single group-level contest with all-or-nothing outcome.

### Decision 5 — Enslave vs Absolute Control — DECIDED: A
Merge them into one top-tier finisher identity. Any legacy names may remain as presentation variants only; do not retain duplicate mechanical records without a real difference in requirements or scaling.

- **A. Merge into one top-tier finisher.**
- B. Keep distinct through different setup/resource loops and declared outcome requests.
- C. Keep both only if they request different permitted outcomes; otherwise merge.

### Decision 6 — Overflow architecture — DECIDED: A
Preserve three distinct roles: S Symbol = passive recovery, Ecstasy Aftershocks = automatic area retaliation, Overflow Engine = active/configurable conversion. All require output caps and recursion guards.

- **A. Keep all three roles distinct.**
- B. Reduce to two roles by merging recovery and active conversion.
- C. Reduce to two roles by merging retaliation and active conversion.

## 8. Still open after this pass

- Numerical scaling curves, target-count formulas, caps, costs, thresholds, and duration.
- Unique mechanical differentiators for Flaunt and G-bang, given the decision to keep them distinct.
- Exact allowed outcome per encounter and per finisher.
- Overflow boundary and event order as reconciled with turn-resolution.
- Exact Strain/Vitality channel and conditional rider for Spank/Bite/Facesit.
- Flirt's combined Command + Strain tuning.
- AS Chain/reset behavior.
- H-job's precise mental/sensory effect.
- P-growth target scope, persistence, and revocation.
- Whether scaling/affinity alone justifies every basic skill's unlock cost.

## 9. Acceptance criteria

Before CSV migration:
- Every area action defines its target set, per-target resolution, target-count scaling, rider scope, friendly fire, and deterministic ordering.
- Every finisher obeys the encounter outcome contract and defines failure/partial success.
- Every Overflow listener has a single trigger contract, bounded output, and recursion behavior.
- No numeric balancing occurs before target semantics and distinctness decisions are closed.


## Historical reconciliation pass 6 (decision trail)

# AHTW — Skill Catalog Reconciliation Pass 6
## Cross-cutting semantics: target resolution, scaling, capabilities, and event atomicity

> **Status:** Updated with the project owner's seven decisions. Pass 4 and Pass 5 decisions remain binding constraints. This pass does not modify `data/skills/skills.csv`, lock numeric coefficients, or finalize the catalog.

## 1. Decisions carried forward

### Pass 4
- Similar basic actions may remain distinct when per-skill body-part affinity/scaling creates meaningful build choices; share effect templates where possible.
- Spank and Bite each have one primary damage channel. A second channel is an explicit conditional rider.
- Flirt generates Command and deals Strain damage; cost, yield, and damage must be balanced together.
- Lay/Facesit self-risk exists only when intentionally defined as a trade-off with a declared payoff.
- AS remains a Chain/sequence skill with explicit reset conditions; if no meaningful Chain behavior can be defined, consolidate with Bend.
- H-job retains Mind as its intended Aspect and must become a genuine mental/sensory interaction.

### Pass 5
- Flaunt, G-bang, and Strip remain mechanically distinct. They need real differentiators beyond labels/animations.
- Summon c-s concentrates output against fewer valid enemies, with a defined curve and cap.
- Summon Tentacles may affect enemies, allies, and the user; each side/effect is declared and resolved deterministically.
- Bkakke allows encounter victory only if every enemy is eligible and every outcome request succeeds; each failed/ineligible target also triggers a defined self-risk consequence, while successful target outcomes persist.
- Enslave and Absolute Control merge into one top-tier finisher identity; no duplicate mechanical records.
- Overflow retains three distinct roles: S Symbol = passive recovery; Ecstasy Aftershocks = automatic area retaliation; Overflow Engine = active/configurable conversion.
- CSV remains unchanged.

## 2. Cross-cutting semantics audit

### 2.1 Action validation and costs
